2027 අය වැය කථාමේ ඉංග්‍රීසි අ අවාවාදය 2027 வரவு கசலவுத்திட்ை உடரயின் ஆங்கிலவாக்கம்

ENGLISH TEXT OF THE BUDGET SPEECH - 2027

1. Preamble

Hon. Speaker, our Government presents its third Budget with the honest and genuine determination of making the story, cherished over many generations, of building a thriving nation to a reality. Every Budget we have presented bears witness to our collective resilience, and has established a firm conviction that, as a nation, we are emerging from the deepest crisis and are on a correct and well-planned trajectory.

We have been able to kindle an unfading dawn of hope in the hearts of hundreds of thousands of our people. I wish to recall that we are unconditionally fulfilling the expectations of the noble trust that the entire people of this country placed in us two years ago.

Eradicating poverty, building a just society, uprooting bribery and corruption, accelerating economic development, restoring the trust of the people and providing every citizen with the opportunity to build a decent life are among our key objectives. However, the foundation for all of these must be the creation, through education, of citizens who are equipped with knowledge, endowed with virtue, think critically and act creatively, as well as visionary leaders. What we need is to build an educated Sri Lankan generation that is rich in knowledge and intellect, contributes to the world through new ideas and new inventions, and is capable of facing any challenge at the international level. Our aim is to create a situation where, wherever in the world, a Sri Lankan is recognized, he or she is accepted as a person endowed with knowledge, intellect, creativity and good virtues.

It is to make the Sri Lankan identity a symbol of intelligence, virtue and excellence. Creating a true renaissance through all of these is the core objective of our Budget. This is because ushering in a renaissance era is not merely a change in economic indicators or statistics, but it is a broad social transformation that brings about a change in people's thinking, knowledge, attitudes and behaviour, and breathes new life into the cultural, intellectual and artistic life of the country.

We must all come together and, through a collective people's intervention, take this country forward towards the development that we, as Sri Lanka in the 21st Century, should have already achieved.

On this journey, the first thing we had to do was to bring about a fundamental transformation in the political culture. For the first time in history since Independence, the people of this country gave us the mandate to establish a genuine people's governance in place of a government administration that had been in the hands of a few corrupt elite families.

What we need is not merely a Government that collects revenue, but a Government that delivers value to the people for every tax rupee it collects. What we need is not a large Government machinery, but an efficient and transparent Government machinery that is accountable to the people.

Our policy is to recognize, at every level of governance, that the true owners of public money are the citizens of this country.

Hon. Speaker, today, we have the confidence, experience, economic foundation and stability required to take our country's economy into a transformative era. The Budget we present for the year 2027 is the next step built on those foundations. Our expectation is to make it a Budget that strengthens the production economy, protects small and medium-scale industrialists, makes room for investment, connects knowledge and technology to the economy through digital economic activities, protects the environment and ensures social justice.

Every Budget Proposal we present is a part of a continuous programme moving towards an interconnected common goal. Moreover, this Budget has not been prepared based solely on the views of one individual or one party, but it is a programme prepared collectively, incorporating the views, proposals and experiences of various stakeholders. Due attention has been paid to every proposal submitted to us. This Budget has been prepared based on our policy priorities, available resources, fiscal space and implementation capacity.

Hon. Speaker, it is no exaggeration to say that this economic stability of our country was built during a most difficult period. The war raging in the Middle East is becoming more prolonged than ever before, creating great uncertainty in the world. The closure of the Strait of Hormuz has affected nearly 20 per cent of global petroleum shipments. Accordingly, the increase in fuel prices, premiums and shipping and insurance charges had a very adverse impact on our energy market. We have still not been able to free ourselves from the pressure this created on our economy and on people's lives. As a result, more than Rs. 147 billion was spent to minimize the impact on the people and on economic stability.

Further, according to the Post-Disaster Needs Assessment - PDNA - Report, the damage caused by Cyclone Ditwah to housing, industries, railway systems, agriculture, education and the health system has been estimated at US Dollars 2 billion, which, together with economic losses of US Dollars 1.4 billion, amounts to a total of about US Dollars 3.4 billion - Rs. 1.022 trillion.

We have been able to build economic stability while facing all these adverse circumstances.

Hon. Speaker, as forecast by the World Meteorological Organization, it has been confirmed that there is a 100 per cent probability that the El Niño weather condition that has now developed will reach its peak in February, 2027. This El Niño weather condition

349 350

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

poses a risk of adverse impacts on agriculture, food prices, water resources and hydropower. We consider it one of our foremost responsibilities to identify such risks that may arise both globally and domestically in advance, and to further stabilize the economy so that it is capable of facing them.

Our target is not only to manage today's economic challenges, but also to transform the country into one where future generations will never again have to face an economic crisis such as the one that prevailed before. The essence of our policy statement, "A Thriving Nation – A Beautiful Life", is not merely to improve the performance of economic indicators, but to create a society in which people can live with dignity and pride, and a country that has confidence in its future.

The main objective of this Budget is to move from economic stability towards transformation. It is to achieve a progressive transformation in every sphere, including investment, exports, productive employment, social welfare and sustainability.

Hon. Speaker, during the past two years, our Government has succeeded in achieving remarkable macroeconomic stability. Accordingly,

• Sustaining the economic growth of 5 per cent

achieved in 2025, an economic growth of 5.1 per cent was achieved in the first quarter of 2026, even amidst the impact of Cyclone Ditwah. In the second quarter, a growth of 4.2 per cent was achieved, despite the war situation in the Middle East region.

• The negative primary balance, which had recorded

over a long period, reported the highest positive balance of 5.4 per cent in 2025 in its history. Against the positive primary balance target of Rs. 360 billion for end-2026, a positive primary balance of Rs. 1,844 billion was recorded by end-September.

• In 2025, the budget deficit recorded its lowest value

in 68 years at 2.3 per cent.

• Government revenue, which was at a historic low of

8.2 per cent of GDP in 2022, reached 16.6 per cent in 2025, the highest value recorded in 23 years, amounting to about 110 per cent of the expected revenue.

• In 2025, we were able to record the highest-ever

Current Account surplus in history of US Dollars 1,719 million.

• In 2025, workers' remittances recorded the highest

value in history at US Dollars 8.1 billion. Further, by October this year, it amounted to US Dollars 7.6 billion. Accordingly, remittances exceeding US Dollars 9 billion are expected this year.

• Central Government debt, which was 114 per cent of

GDP at the end of 2022, was reduced to 95 per cent by the end of 2025 as per the records of Public Debt Management Office – PDMO.

• In 2022, 79.1 per cent of Government revenue was

spent on interest payments, which declined further to 45.9 per cent by 2025 and to 39.6 per cent by September 2026.

• The total net loss before tax of Rs. 774.6 billion

recorded by 52 key State-Owned Enterprises in 2022 was turned into a profit of Rs. 444.4 billion in 2025. This reduced the pressure on the Treasury. We turned loss-making institutions into profit-making institutions. By the end of August, 2026, a profit before tax of Rs. 367 billion was recorded. The accumulated loss of SriLankan Airlines from the date it was taken back in 2008 up to 2025 amounts to Rs. 528 billion. Accordingly, SriLankan Airlines has become a burden on the General Treasury. The Cabinet of Ministers has already appointed an expert committee to find the necessary solutions in this regard.

• Even though total fuel import expenditure increased

to US Dollars 4,487 million by September, 2026 compared to US Dollars 4,042 million in 2025 due to the increase in world market fuel prices, and amidst the opening of the vehicle import market, foreign reserves were recorded at over US Dollars 7 billion by October, 2026.

• Nevertheless, the annual inflation, which was

recorded at 2.1 per cent in December 2025, increased to 8.3 per cent by October, 2026 due to the rise in fuel prices in the face of the Middle East conflict and other related factors.

• Credit extended by commercial banks to the private

sector increased by 25.2 per cent, from Rs. 8,156 billion in 2024 to Rs. 10,212 billion in 2025.

Hon. Speaker, during the past two years, Sri Lanka has been able to achieve significant progress in global rankings, including economic and social rankings.

• According to the 2026 Fitch Ratings, Sri Lanka has

been upgraded from 'CCC+' to 'B-'.

• According to the Corruption Perceptions Index

(2025/2026) issued by Transparency International, Sri Lanka has advanced 14 places in the global rankings to 107th place.

• According to the Global Peace Index (2026), Sri

Lanka advanced 14 places to be ranked 67th globally.

• According to GlobeTrotter & Flash Pack, in 2025,

Sri Lanka was ranked first in the world index under the solo travellers category.

• The World Economic Forum has named Sri Lanka

10th in the world among the countries that have shown the fastest progress among tourism economies.

• The international magazine “Condé Nast” has named

Sri Lanka the best destination in the world for the whole family to travel together.

351 352

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

• According to the assessment carried out by the

Institute of International Finance - IIF -, among 57 emerging market and developing economies, Sri Lanka secured fourth place for progress in improving investor relations and fifth place for progress in improving transparency on public debt.

• The World Bank had classified Sri Lanka as a lower

middle-income country in 2020, down from the status of an upper middle-income country. Reflecting the recovery of the economy, the classification was upgraded again in 2026 to an upper middle-income country.

Hon. Speaker, the above facts are not the only criteria that measure the success of our fiscal policy. The Government's support for and care of distressed children, the elderly, persons with disabilities, those suffering from chronic illnesses, low-income households and families who have lost their livelihoods due to disasters are equally important.

Through the 2025 Budget, we laid the foundation for economic stability and social justice. Through the 2026 Budget, greater attention was paid to production, investment, digitalization and institutional reforms. What we propose through the 2027 Budget is to implement the reforms and proposals for a new economic transformation required, based on the economic stability we have achieved so far, to increase production, to involve the people as stakeholders engaged in the economy, to ensure that the gains achieved in the economy are distributed fairly among the people, and to achieve the sovereignty of the national economy.

Economic transformation does not mean presenting yet another list of expenditures. It means that every investment made by the Government, every tax concession granted, every borrowing undertaken and every social protection programme proposed will be assessed on the basis of the economic and social benefits generated through them.

It is not our objective to make all the sacrifices that the people bore during the economic crisis, and continue to bear even at this moment, into permanent sacrifices. It is our duty to build economic stability, better jobs, fair incomes, a quality Public Service, a safe social environment and a sustainable future for future generations.

2. Seven Strategic Objectives of the 2027

Budget

Hon. Speaker, when the Budget for 2026 was presented to this House, we emphasized the need to build an economy that can move forward unshaken in the face of internal and external challenges. At the end of 2025, Cyclone Ditwah severely affected the lives of our people, production activities and infrastructure. Due to the Middle East conflict that escalated in the first half of 2026, new pressure exerted on energy prices, import costs and supply chains.

In facing a disaster such as Cyclone Ditwah, what was important for the Government was that we had built fiscal space in advance. Through this, we were able to strengthen the financial capacity required to provide relief to the people affected by the disaster, to maintain essential services and to deploy resources for reconstruction activities according to priorities.

The fundamental structural macroeconomic problem that Sri Lanka has faced for a long time has been the deficits in the budget, the balance of payments and the Current Account. Although we were able to reduce the budget deficit significantly through correct revenue and expenditure policies, the external sector has not yet been strengthened as required. While a Current Account surplus of US Dollars 1,853 million was recorded in the first nine months of 2025, a deficit of US Dollars 420 million was recorded in the corresponding period of 2026. The progress achieved in the fiscal sector must be made the foundation for a structural transformation that strengthens the external sector.

This need becomes even clearer when we look at the current state of the world economy. According to the World Economic Outlook issued by the International Monetary Fund in October, 2026, global economic growth is expected to slow down to 3.1 per cent in 2026. Risks such as the escalation of conflicts, rising energy and food prices and uncertainty in international trade still persist.

We need a long-term plan capable of withstanding these conditions.

2.1 Safeguarding Macroeconomic Stability and

Strengthening the Resilience of the Economy

While safeguarding macroeconomic and fiscal stability, our main objective is to ensure debt sustainability and to prepare Sri Lanka to face future internal and external shocks. For this, sustainable Government revenue, prudent expenditure management, planned debt repayments, adequate fiscal space and strong foreign reserves are essential. It is also important to maintain adequate coordination between fiscal and monetary policies, while respecting the legal mandate of the Central Bank of Sri Lanka in maintaining price stability.

The Central Government debt stood at 114 per cent in 2022, the year in which the economy was plunged into crisis. What is taken into consideration in debt sustainability is the capacity to repay debt relative to GDP and Government revenue, and whether there is adequate liquidity to settle the debt servicing obligations falling due in the near term.

The target of the IMF-Extended Fund Facility programme is to bring public debt down to a level of 95 per cent by 2032. Based on the progress in public financial management, the IMF 6th Review has forecast that this will decline to 87 per cent. Annual borrowing plans and strategies are being implemented under the leadership of the Public Debt Management Office to

353 354

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

maintain debt sustainability. In 2025, the Central Government debt ratio had reached 95 per cent. Total foreign debt service amounted to US Dollars 3,707 million in 2025 and US Dollars 3,591 million in 2026. Going forward, it will be US Dollars 2,986 million and US Dollars 3,574 million in 2027 and 2028, respectively. We have the stability required for this debt servicing.

2.2 Accelerating Economic Growth Driven by

Investment, Production and Exports

Our target is to accelerate the structural transformation towards a high-productivity, export-competitive production economy. Expanding domestic and foreign private investment, diversifying export products and markets, integrating with international value chains and promoting industries and services with higher value addition are the main areas of this.

We are taking steps to broaden access to finance for small and medium-scale enterprises and to establish a clear and transparent investment environment for investors.

2.3 Enhancing Productivity through Technology,

Innovation and Human Capital

We give priority to enhancing national productivity by strengthening education, competency development, research, innovation and digital transformation, modernizing public services, expanding digital infrastructure, encouraging the use of new technologies and strengthening collaboration among educational institutions, research institutions and industries.

In particular, it is essential to equip our younger generation with the competencies required for the changing domestic and global job market, and to transform knowledge and innovation into income-generating enterprises and services. Creating a technologically capable workforce and a more productive economy will also enhance export competitiveness and the capacity to earn foreign exchange.

2.4 Empowering the Rural Economy and Ensuring Inclusive Growth

The agri-food sector has very high potential for export and rural income growth. Instead of excessive reliance on broad-based and untargeted subsidies that do not contribute to raising productivity, high priority should be given to directing unproductive lands to productive cultivation, agricultural research, climate-smart technology, water management, storage and cold storage facilities, product quality certification and digital systems. It is also essential to resolve, after proper study, the existing barriers to land use, rural access to finance and connecting producers to markets. Then, it will be possible to make the empowerment of the livelihoods of rural people, food security and foreign exchange earnings the outcomes of a single development process.

A new economic transformation at the rural level can be brought about by encouraging small and medium-scale entrepreneurs, creating new economic opportunities for rural people, and developing the technical, vocational and linguistic skills required for the modern job market.

2.5 Stability and Continuity of the Energy

Markets

Rising international fuel prices, supply chain disruptions and climatic changes form the basis of instability in the energy sector in our country. Our objective is to change these underlying conditions that we face with regard to energy. For this, it is also necessary to expand renewable energy, and to improve electricity transmission networks, energy storage and energy efficiency. Our target is to minimize the long-term pressure on foreign exchange spent on imported fuel, while safeguarding the continuity of essential energy supply through the use of diverse supply routes and energy sources.

2.6 Strengthening Resilience to Climate Disasters

It is important to follow standards capable of withstanding future disasters, early warnings and proper maintenance plans. Along with this, food supply chains, storage, public transport and the mechanisms for distributing essential goods must be strengthened. The continuity of services such as essential communication facilities and electricity must be ensured. Although the risks arising from natural disasters that may occur in the future cannot be completely eliminated, our objective is to significantly minimize the resulting paralysis of economic activities and the pressure on people's lives.

2.7 Modernizing the Government Machinery

It is essential to build an efficient Government machinery that provides a quality service to the people, facilitates investment and production activities, and is accountable to the law and transparent. Modernizing public financial management, State-owned Enterprise and asset management, tax administration and the procurement process are essential elements of this. Minimizing corruption and unnecessary administrative delays, while strengthening the rule of law and accountability, is equally important to enhance public trust and to safeguard public resources.

3. Medium-Term Macroeconomic and Fiscal

Path

Hon. Speaker, our medium-term economic target is to move towards a sustainable economic growth of 7 per cent or higher. A strong and credible fiscal foundation is essential for the economic transformation we envisage. Accordingly, we expect to continue fiscal consolidation in line with the responsibility framework established by the "Medium-Term Fiscal Framework 2027–2031" and the Public Financial Management Act. Maintaining a primary surplus of 2.3 per cent of GDP, maintaining the budget deficit at a level below 5 per cent, and placing public debt on a sustainable path are our core fiscal targets.

355 356

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

Our objective is to maintain Government revenue at a level not less than 16 per cent of GDP in the medium term, and to gradually increase it to about 20 per cent in the long term. To achieve these targets, the necessary reforms will be implemented to broaden the tax base, rationalize tax exemptions and incentives, digitalize tax administration and strengthen tax compliance.

In accordance with the Public Financial Management Act, we will take steps to maintain medium-term expenditure in line with the primary expenditure ceiling. However, due to the additional allocations required to restore the infrastructure and people's livelihood damaged by Cyclone Ditwah, primary expenditure in 2027 is expected to increase temporarily to 13.6 per cent of GDP. While managing these special expenditure requirements within the statutory fiscal framework, we also expect to bring primary expenditure back to 13 per cent of GDP from 2028 onwards.

Hon. Speaker, we expect to maintain public investment at a level exceeding 4 per cent of GDP from 2027 onwards. In 2027, a Capital Expenditure of Rs. 2,000 billion has been allocated, amounting to total public investment of 5.12 per cent of GDP, comprising 4.48 per cent through the Budget and 0.64 per cent through the Supplementary Estimate for Ditwah.

The Extended Fund Facility programme entered into with the International Monetary Fund - IMF - is scheduled to conclude in March next year. We expect to successfully complete the 17th IMF programme. While the programme to stabilize the economy domestically is proceeding successfully, it is difficult to forecast the impacts arising from global and natural disasters at a time when we are preparing for an economic transformation for growth. We believe that our reserves must be further strengthened to successfully face such situations.

Therefore, the Cabinet of Ministers has appointed a committee headed by the Deputy Minister of Finance and Planning to examine and submit a report on the suitability of entering into another programme after the successful completion of the current IMF programme, and on what type of programme it should be.

For social justice and the protection of the people, targeted social protection programmes will be further strengthened to protect low-income and vulnerable households who face the highest risk in the face of economic and natural disasters.

Hon. Speaker, our people made exceptional sacrifices and endured great hardships for this economic stabilization. We must all pay our respect to them. Doing justice to these sacrifices means achieving a rapid turn from economic stabilization towards transformation. To that end, I present to this honourable House the Budget Proposals for the year 2027.

Building the Essential Foundations for Economic Transformation

4. Strengthening the Legal Framework

A new and strong legal framework and an institutional structure are required to achieve the economic transformation we envisage and to enhance efficiency. Some of our laws are more than 100 years old. The historical role of some of our institutions has come to an end. Those laws and institutions have become shackles that bind our feet from reaching the economic transformation we envisage. Therefore, in order to achieve the new economic transformation, it is necessary to reformulate old and outdated laws and institutions so that they can face present-day challenges, and to build new laws and institutions that correspond to the new circumstances that have arisen. Hence, we cannot move forward without removing these shackles. Accordingly,

• The Rescue, Rehabilitation and Insolvency (Corporate and Personal) Act, No. 12 of 2026 has been passed to provide an opportunity for businesses that collapsed due to various crises such as pandemics, natural disasters and terrorist acts to rebuild, contribute to the economy and repay their creditors.

• The laws required to strengthen the prevention of

money laundering have been passed through the Prevention of Money Laundering (Amendment) Act, No. 16 of 2026.

• The Financial Transactions Reporting (Amendment)

Act, No. 17 of 2026 has made provisions to minimize the risk arising from transactions not being reported to the financial system and to enforce the law against non-compliers.

• Through the Convention on the Suppression of

Terrorist Financing (Amendment) Act, No. 18 of 2026, the financing of global terrorism using Sri Lanka, which poses a threat to national security, has been prevented by strengthening the financial system.

• Many people who have obtained microfinance loans

are still stranded in a severe debt trap. For their protection, the Microfinance and Credit Regulatory Authority Act, No. 9 of 2026 had already been passed. Accordingly, the Microfinance and Credit Regulatory Authority will be established in 2027 and steps will be taken to provide protection to business customers.

• The Public-Private Partnership (PPP) Act has already

been published in the Gazette for the initiation, development, management and monitoring of public-private partnership projects. It is expected to be presented to Parliament in early 2027.

• The draft State-owned Business Enterprises Management Bill has already been referred to the Attorney-General to incorporate a holding company for the efficient management of companies, in which the State holds shares. 34 State-owned enterprises have already been identified for this purpose.

357 358

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

• The draft Investment Protection Bill has already been

referred to the Attorney General to promote and protect investments and assets belonging to investors, to ensure fair and equitable treatment and to make provisions relating to the payment of compensation and the settlement of disputes.

• The draft Colombo Port City Economic Commission

(Amendment) Bill has been referred to the Attorney-General to attract foreign investment to the Colombo Port City and to provide greater facilities for investors and those employed there.

• The Institute of Professional Actuaries of Sri Lanka

Act required to establish a new institute aimed at ensuring the quality of professional actuaries and improving professional training is being drafted.

• Data and information are most important in policy

decision-making in this era. The National Statistics Act is being drafted for the preparation, coordination and governance of official statistics in Sri Lanka.

• The Customs Ordinance (Amendment) Act is

expected to be passed in the first half of 2027 with the aim of making the Sri Lanka Customs more efficient and transparent and facilitating businesses. From October this year, the Sri Lanka Customs has implemented a paperless programme and has digitalized the Customs Long Room, one of its divisions that had been in use for about a century.

• The Financial Reporting Regulatory (Amendment)

Act will be amended to formalize the Sri Lanka Accounting Standards, Sri Lanka Auditing Standards and the regulatory standards on quality control.

• The Excise (Special Provisions) Act will be amended

to provide solutions to long-standing issues that have arisen in its implementation.

• The Finance Business Act will be amended to

strengthen the regulation and supervision of finance companies, the investigation and prosecution of unauthorized finance businesses, and the requirements for winding up non-viable finance businesses.

• The Public Asset Management Act will be introduced

to meet the requirements of public asset management.

• The Cabinet of Ministers has appointed an expert

committee to reform labour laws that have been in place for decades, so as to align them with current social and economic needs. This initiative aims to create a conducive working environment required for investment and production processes to be carried out in an effective and efficient manner and to promote employer-employee relations, in which professional responsibility and accountability are clearly defined. This committee is in the process of formulating recommendations, taking into account the views and proposals of relevant stakeholders, including the National Labour Advisory Council.

Essential Structural Changes to be Made

• An officials' committee has been appointed by the

Cabinet of Ministers to look into minimizing delays in providing Government lands to investors, and to submit recommendations on the concessions to be granted according to the national importance of the project, the number of jobs generated and the area in which the investment is made.

• It is expected to establish a company, 100 per cent

owned by the Treasury, comprising professionals with private sector and international experience, for the promotion of investment. It is expected to provide this company too with market-competitive salaries and allowances, as was considered in the establishment of the GovTech institution.

• The Gambling Regulatory Authority has already

been established to regulate casinos, online gambling and other games that fall into the category of gambling. The necessary regulations have already been finalized with expert assistance.

• The National Single Window for Trade, which has

been launched as a pilot project from the fifth of this month for the export of several selected goods, is expected to connect exporters as the first phase in the first half of 2027, and importers and re-exports in the second half of 2027. Rs. 1,990 million has been allocated for this purpose.

• Bids have been called to obtain a consultancy firm

for the creation of the National Single Window for Investment, and these bids are currently being evaluated. Rs. 100 million has been allocated for its establishment.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million for the

procurement of body scanners and container scanners, aimed at expanding the scanning system to strengthen the border protection process of Sri Lanka Customs.

• It is proposed to modernize the existing Temporary

Importation for Export Processing - TIEP - process, under which goods imported for export are cleared with tax concessions, in order to introduce "inter-port trading" mechanisms and to direct small and medium-scale industrialists towards exports.

5. Free Trade Agreements

We expect to update the existing Free Trade Agreements and enter into new trade agreements with more countries in order to connect Sri Lanka to the global trade network.

• An officials' committee has already been appointed by the Cabinet of Ministers to expand the existing Free Trade Agreement with India.

• Attention has been paid to a new Free Trade

Agreement with China.

• An application has been made to accede to the

Regional Comprehensive Economic Partnership - RCEP.

359 360

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

• It is expected to enter into Comprehensive

Economic Partnership Agreements with the United Arab Emirates and Oman.

• The Sri Lanka–European Union Sustainable Trade

and Economic Integration Project will be implemented under a European Union grant of Rs. 3,430 million during the period 2027-2030 to promote export competitiveness, export diversification and sustainable production.

Further, we will work to enter into new Free Trade Agreements with other countries following a feasibility study.

6. Energy

A transformative change in the energy sector is required for the future economic transformation, national security and the improvement of the living standards of the people. The plans required for a new transformation in the energy sector, aimed at ensuring a stable and uninterrupted energy supply and reducing production costs, have been included in this Budget.

6.1 Electricity

Today, around 62 per cent of our installed capacity comes from renewable energy. Plans have been prepared to increase this to 69 per cent by 2029.

As energy sources that vary with weather and seasonal conditions, such as solar and wind power, are increasingly added, we must pay greater attention to the stability control of the system. The national power system failures that occurred in February, 2025 and October, 2026 were an important lesson for us to reflect on our system protection, frequency control, power flow management and network protection mechanisms. We expect to make an investment of Rs. 26.5 billion to technically strengthen our grid system so that such incidents do not recur.

• The installation of two synchronous condensers of 75

MVA (megavolt-amperes) each at the new Habarana and Mannar locations, which will help maintain grid stability when renewable energy is added to the national system at a high percentage, and the installation of STATCOM facilities with a capacity of 300 MVAr at the Padukka 220 kV grid substation are expected to commence in 2027 and be completed in 2029 with the financial assistance of the World Bank.

• A Wide Area Monitoring System will be established

under the World Bank Technical Assistance Project to identify unstable conditions in the system in advance and to make prompt and effective decisions in emergencies.

• We have already introduced Battery Energy Storage

Systems – BESS - to store the surplus electricity we receive during the daytime. In 2026, 160 MW is received by the system through BESS. Plans have been made for this quantity to be 560 MW in 2027 and for the total quantity to be 1,245 MW by 2029.

• The price selected in the first phase (160 MW) of

calling bids for the BESS system in 2026 was Rs. 2.033 million per megawatt per month. In the second phase in 2026, when bids were called for 250 megawatts, the bid received had decreased to Rs. 1.808 million per megawatt per month. Accordingly, through competitive bidding, it has been possible to reduce the price by around 11 per cent within this year itself.

• With the aim of promoting renewable energy

generation, enhancing energy security, and ensuring stability by minimizing imbalances in the national power system, it is proposed to provide a subsidy for the purchase of the batteries required for energy storage to domestic consumers who install rooftop solar power systems equipped with battery storage facilities under the Net Plus scheme.

• Domestic consumers who newly install rooftop solar

power systems will be provided a subsidy of Rs. 25,000 per kilowatt of battery storage capacity. It is proposed to provide this subsidy for up to 10 kilowatts, subject to a maximum of Rs. 250,000. It is proposed to provide Rs. 3,500 million for this purpose. It is also proposed to remove the Ports and Airports Development Levy - PAL - charged on the importation of batteries used for the storage of solar energy.

• Although the need for supplementary power supply

in the event of an emergency at the Norochcholai Lakvijaya Power Plant has been discussed for a long time, no appropriate steps have been taken to address it. Measures are now being taken to establish a BESS with a capacity of 30 MW to provide this supplementary power.

• Rs. 500 million has been allocated for the preliminary

work of the 400 kV northern main transmission network from Kilinochchi to New Habarana, with the aim of completing it by 2030. We expect to obtain financial assistance of US Dollars 150 million from the Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank – AIIB - to construct the transmission line.

• It is expected to invite private investments for the

construction of two transmission lines of 220 kV from Norochcholai to Wariyapola and from Monaragala to Hambantota, and the New Habarana–Kirindiwela 400 kV transmission line.

• In 2027, smart meters will be provided free of charge

to 300,000 electricity consumers who use more than 180 units on a grant of US Dollars 12 million from the Asian Development Bank - ADB. Smart meters will be provided to all electricity consumers who use more than 120 units in the areas served by Lanka Electricity Company - LECO.

• A new capacity of 370 MW generated from wind

power will be added to the system by 2029. Of this, steps will be taken to add 50 MW in 2027. By the end of 2029, the installed capacity generated from wind power will increase to 674 MW.

361 362

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

• Ten reservoirs, including Randenigala, Deduru Oya,

Ridiyagama, Ambalan Oya, Castlereigh, Chandrika Wewa and Kiri Ibban Ara, have been selected for the installation of floating solar panels. We expect to generate around 500 MW of electricity by installing solar panels using 10 per cent or less of the water surface of these reservoirs. These projects will be carried out by the Government and the private sector. Steps will be taken in January, 2027 to call for investment proposals from the private sector.

• Four Distribution Control Centres will be established

in Kuliyapitiya, Peradeniya, Battaramulla and Dehiwala to manage power failures, optimize the power grid, manage electricity demand, integrate and manage solar energy resources and other distributed energy resources into the power system and strengthen data-driven planning of electricity distribution systems.

6.2 Petroleum

• The Petroleum Development Authority of Sri Lanka -

PDASL - has called for bids for oil and natural gas exploration and production (Sri Lanka Licencing Round 2026) in four blocks in the Mannar Basin, located off the western coast. The bids are expected to be opened on February 2, 2027.

• To increase fuel storage capacity, six tanks with a

capacity of 64,000 m3 are being constructed at the Kolonnawa Petroleum Complex with an investment of Rs. 3,746 million in 2025, two tanks with a capacity of 22,000 m3 with an investment of Rs. 1,446 million at the end of this year, and 3 tanks with a capacity of 40,000 m3 at the Muthurajawela Storage Terminal with an investment of Rs. 3,786 million in September, 2026. Accordingly, our storage capacity will increase by 126,000 m3, up to the requirement for 45 days.

• Bids have been called for the construction of a

pipeline system from Kolonnawa to the Port with an investment of Rs. 16,332 million, with the aim of reducing the time taken to unload oil from ships from 96 hours to 48 hours. This is expected to be completed within 18 months.

• In line with the proposal to develop the Bandaranaike

International Airport, Katunayake as a hub serving the international aviation sector, construction work has already commenced on a tank complex with a capacity of 92,000 m3 for Jet A1 fuel at the Muthurajawela Petroleum Terminal and a pipeline to carry aviation fuel from Muthurajawela to the Bandaranaike International Airport, Katunayake, with an investment of Rs. 22,340 million.

• The Sapugaskanda Oil Refinery will be upgraded

from its current limit of 50,000 barrels per day to a capacity of 100,000 barrels under a public-private partnership. For this, Requests for Proposals – RFP - will be called in 2027 based on the Expressions of Interest – EoIs - already called.

7. Digital Transformation and Development

towards a Digital Era

Hon. Speaker, digital transformation is no longer merely a technological initiative, but a fundamental requirement for improving the efficiency of the public sector, strengthening governance, facilitating economic growth and ensuring that every citizen can access Government services. Digitalization has been identified as a fundamental tool for modernizing public administration, improving the efficiency and transparency of Government activities and strengthening institutional capacity.

Reaching the level of development this sector has attained in today's world is a challenging and multifaceted process. At the same time, this sector is constantly evolving. Therefore, in order to reap its true benefits, the necessary legal and institutional structure must first be built.

• The GovTech institution has been established under

100 per cent ownership of the Treasury to guide and implement the digitalization of public services, and has already commenced its operations.

• The Personal Data Protection Act has been amended

and the Data Protection Authority has already been established and made operational.

• The Bill for the establishment of the Digital

Economy Authority and the National Digital Economy Council - Digital Economy Bill - is expected to be passed in the first quarter of 2027.

• The Cyber Security Bill, which is essential for

protection with the growth of the digital sector, has been submitted to the Attorney-General for clearance. Accordingly, it is expected to establish a Cyber Security Authority under it in 2027.

• There is a problematic situation in the Government

procurement process when purchasing digital solutions and facilities. Therefore, Government P rocurement Guidelines specific to digital solutions and facilities have been prepared, and discussions have already commenced with the Procurement Commission. Accordingly, it will be possible to introduce these procurement guidelines in the first half of 2027.

For a new transformation in the digital economy sector;

• Establishing a National Artificial Intelligence Centre

of Excellence at Wijerama Mawatha, bringing the Government, the expert community and industry under one roof to build national AI capabilities and promote the responsible use of AI;

• Establishing the "Shared Platform First and Buy

Before Build Policy", which, in parallel with the standard for integrating public institutions through digital public infrastructure, gives priority to purchasing standardized solutions and to shared use, before developing new software in software procurement;

363 364

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

• Expanding the Lanka Government Cloud – LGC -

system from LGC 2.0 to LGC 3.0 and establishing the core foundation of sovereign infrastructure for Artificial Intelligence;

• Digitalizing the services provided by Divisional

Secretariats and Grama Niladharis so that citizens can easily access Government services;

• Launching the "Presidential Digital Excellence

Awards" programme to appriase high-performing public institutions with the aim of rapidly promoting digitalization;

• Adopting and expanding cashless payment methods

such as GovPay and QR to steer towards a digital revenue economy;

• Further, preparing the necessary regulatory basis for

Virtual Asset Service Providers – VASP - through the establishment of a formal legal framework;

• Preparing sectoral digital plans to align the

digitalization of key strategic sectors with the National Digital Economy Plan, and carrying out operations through institutional Digital Transformation Units - DTUs;

• Providing electricity tariff concessions and other

incentives to encourage investors selected under formal criteria, with the expectation of encouraging investment in the establishment of data centres;

• Providing broadband vouchers to 750,000 selected

school students to enhance digital literacy among them;

• Strengthening the Digital Startup Fund of Funds to

attract private investment into Sri Lankan businesses;

It is proposed to allocate Rs. 10,000 million for the above Proposals.

Rs. 18,500 million has already been allocated to the Ministry of Digital Economy for the year 2027 to support the digital transformation and the development of the digital economy of the country under six key areas,namely,1)institutional strengthening, policy and regulation, 2) digital Government transformation, 3) digital industry development and growth, 4) empowering the digital revenue economy, 5) empowering digital activities in strategic industries, and 6) promoting inclusive access and adoption, and capacity development, and this amount will be increased to Rs. 28,500 million.

• Regulations were formulated to remove the Cellular

Tower Levy on new telecommunication towers for a period of five years, which was among the proposals I presented in the 2026 Budget. While expanding high-speed Internet facilities within the country through the further successful implementation of that programme, there is a requirement for between 700 - 800 telecommunication towers to expand high-speed broadband internet coverage Island-wide under the "Gamata Sannivedanaya" Programme by 2030. Of these, 573 telecommunication towers will be completed by 2028. A grant of 75 per cent of the cost

incurred for a new tower constructed or Rs. 35 million, whichever is lower, will be provided. In addition, Rs. 5 million will be provided for obtaining an electricity connection. Rs. 9,000 million has been allocated for this purpose by the Telecommunications Regulatory Commission through the "Gamata Sannivedanaya" Programme.

• At present, there are problems with the Quality of

Service and Quality of Experience in the mobile (broadband) connectivity networks in the country, and rural people in particular face great difficulty. To identify and correct these accurately, the Telecommunications Regulatory Commission will commence the introduction of an automated monitoring system in 2027 for this purpose, for the first time in Sri Lanka, through which it is expected to improve the Quality of Service and Quality of Experience of the networks of mobile (broadband) connectivity providers in the country. With the aim of correcting the financial loss incurred by the country as a result of the frequency spectrum belonging to the people of this country being issued without effective and efficient management, the installation of an Automated Spectrum Management System and an Automated Spectrum Monitoring System will commence in 2027. The Telecommunications Regulatory Commission has allocated Rs. 3,100 million for the procurement of all these systems.

• Steps will be taken to rapidly expand the opportunity

for citizens to obtain services without physically visiting Government institutions - presence-less services. Accordingly, from 2027 onwards, the range of priority public services relating to licences, applications, approvals and payments that are widely obtained by the public, from the village level to Divisional Secretariats, Local Government institutions and the Central Government level, will be gradually expanded to be delivered through digital means. With the introduction of the National Digital Identity - Digital ID - and the expansion of its use in the future, it is expected to enhance these services rapidly and sustainably and to extend them across the entire Government machinery.

Our objective is to build an efficient Public Service and a competitive digital economy, while making the daily lives of the people easier through digital technology.

8. Education

Education holds the greatest responsibility in building the human resources required for the modern world. Therefore, a qualitative transformation in education as well as a new technological transformation in the delivery of education is required. For this, it is essential to develop the necessary infrastructure and human resources.

• The new education reforms have already commenced

for Grade 1 and will commence for Grades 2 and 6 next year.

365 366

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

• To ease the financial burden on trainees undergoing

teacher training at the Colleges of Education, it is proposed to increase the monthly allowance paid to them from Rs. 10,500 to Rs. 20,000 with effect from April, 2027. This is expected to serve as financial support during their teacher training period, as well as to help prepare them to assume the future responsibilities of our education system.

• To support students pursuing their education at

Pirivenas and to help meet their educational needs, we are taking steps to increase the daily allowance provided to student monks from Rs. 15 to Rs. 50, and the daily allowance for lay students from Rs. 5 to Rs. 25, with effect from April, 2027.

• With the aim of producing a generation of children

endowed with values, morality and virtue, steps will be taken to increase by Rs. 5,000, from Rs. 7,500 to Rs. 12,500, the annual allowance currently paid from 2027 to Dhamma School teachers who contribute voluntarily to safeguard the quality of Dhamma School education.

• Steps will be taken to provide the school uniforms

donated by the Government of China in the same manner next year as well. The Government of China has expressed its agreement to provide this donation for the next few years as well.

8.1 Digital Transformation Programme in School

Education

• Our target is to provide reliable Internet connections

to all secondary schools. At present, reliable Internet connections have been provided to 6,225 schools, including 3,009 connections provided this year. Steps will be taken to provide connections to the remaining 750 schools expeditiously. This programme will also be launched for primary schools.

• Providing equipment, including computers and smart

boards, required for digital teaching, developing Information Technology and distance learning centres, improving the National Education Management Information System – NEMIS - , providing accessible, interactive digital learning facilities for students with disabilities and enhancing the digital literacy and technical skills of teachers are included in this five-year programme. An estimate of Rs. 96,500 million has been made for these programmes, and it is proposed to allocate a further Rs. 3,000 million in addition to the Rs. 4,000 million allocated this year for its commencement.

• Accordingly, Rs. 35,000 million has been allocated in

2027 for digitalization, the implementation of teacher capacity development programmes and the development of other infrastructure, including the development and modernization of Teachers' Colleges, National Colleges of Education, school buildings, school laboratories and teachers' hostels.

8.2. Higher Education

• Rs. 450 million has been allocated for 2027 for the

construction of fully-fledged Faculties of Medicine and clinical buildings at the Universities of Peradeniya and Wayamba, with an investment of Rs. 8,000 million.

• Rs. 2,250 million has been allocated for the

• Rs. 6,250 million has been allocated for ongoing

student welfare work, including Rs. 717 million for development programmes such as improving the existing hostel facilities at the Hardy Advanced Technological Institute and the Universities of Moratuwa and Sri Jayewardenepura, providing clean drinking water and sanitation facilities for the Kilinochchi premises of the University of Jaffna, and developing wastewater treatment systems at the Universities of Sri Jayewardenepura, Eastern and Jaffna.

• It is proposed to commence a Rs. 2,050 million

official quarters project in 2027 to provide residential facilities for the academic and non-academic staff of the University of Vavuniya.

• Rs. 6,150 million has been estimated for the

restoration of the damage caused to the infrastructure of the University of Peradeniya by Cyclone "Ditwah". In 2027, Rs. 700 million has been allocated for restoration work in higher educational institutions, including this.

• Although approximately 42,000 students are admitted to government universities annually, many students who qualify for university entry are denied this opportunity due to limited resources. With the objective of providing higher education opportunities for these students, the government has implemented a programme, facilitated through State banks, that offers interest-free loans to eligible students, enabling them to pursue degree programs at private higher education institutions. It is proposed to increase the monthly subsistence allowance currently available to these students from Rs. 6,250 to Rs. 10,000, effective from April, 2027.

9. Towards an Economic Transformation

through Research, Development and Innovation

Hon. Speaker, the National Council for Research and Development has been established for policy guidance in the field of science and technology and for directing and coordinating research activities. Through this, its main function is to identify all national research - science, technology, engineering, agriculture, medicine - and to work by giving priority to research that contributes to the production economy. This will not be limited to science and technology activities, but will also undertake research relating to defence and social welfare activities.

Through this,

• Monitoring and regulating research and development

activities carried out by the public as well as the private sector.

• Directing research in a manner that contributes to

national development goals and economic growth.

• Developing the capabilities of local researchers

and supporting advanced research of international standard.

• Transforming the results of research carried out in

laboratories and universities into products or services useful to society and the industrial sector.

• Preparing national research and development policies

to suit the future needs of the country.

• Taking new technologies discovered through research to commercialization and integrating them into the country's economy.

The National Innovation Agency, Sri Lanka Inventors Commission, the National Science Foundation and the National Research Council, the National Science and Technology Commission, and the Sri Lanka Council for Agricultural Research Policy, which come under the Ministry of Science and Technology, are scheduled to be incorporated into this National Council for Research and Development.

Steps will also be taken to provide the institutional, technical and infrastructure facilities necessary to carry out research covering various subject areas through research institutions and other public institutions under various Ministries. Through Government investment, Rs. 23,800 million has been allocated for research and development programmes in 2027.

Rs. 7,600 million, including operational expenses, has been allocated to coordinate all such research activities under the Ministry of Science and Technology.

The National Research and Development Bill has been drafted. It is expected to be presented to Parliament in the first half of 2027.

10. Development of Expressways and Highways

Through this Budget, we expect to make the largest investment in the development of highways, expressways and railways. Rs. 251 billion has been allocated for this purpose in this Budget. Further, we expect to spend Rs. 487 billion, including Rs. 146 billion as new proposals and Rs. 90 billion from the Ditwah Supplementary Estimate for the restoration of damage caused to railways, highways and bridges by Cyclone “Ditwah”. Mainly;

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 30,000 million for the

construction from Kurunegala to Galewela as the first step of the Kurunegala–Dambulla expressway section, which is the 4th phase of the Central Expressway.

• An amount of Rs. 18,680 million has been allocated

in 2026 for the initial activities of the development of the first phase of the Ruwanpura Expressway from Kahathuduwa to Ingiriya, and it is proposed to allocate Rs. 27,000 million in 2027.

• Rs. 53,450 million has been allocated for the year

2027 with the aim of completing the Kadawatha–Mirigama section of the Central Expressway by September, 2028, which had been stalled due to the collapse of the economy.

• Rs. 4,500 million has been allocated for the year

2027 with the aim of completing the Pothuhera–Rambukkana section of the Central Expressway by May 2027.

• Rs. 23,050 million has been allocated for the year

2027 with the aim of completing the Rambukkana–Galagedara section of the Central Expressway, which commenced in 2026, by July, 2029.

It is proposed to allocate Rs. 500 million for the development of connecting roads associated with the Kurunegala–Dambulla Expressway, and Rs. 5,000 million and Rs. 3,000 million, respectively, for the development and rehabilitation of connecting roads associated with the Pothuhera–Galagedara and Kadawatha–Mirigama sections of the Central Expressway.

11. Railway Electrification

• The development of the railway sector, which is

considered as the backbone of passenger transport, has been identified as a Government’s priority. For the implementation of the first phase of this programme, for the year 2027; ° Rs. 45,000 million for the electrification of the Kelani Valley railway line from Maradana to Homagama; ° Rs. 16,400 million for the electrification of the main line from Maradana to Ragama; ° Rs. 17,700 million for the electrification of the coastal line from Maradana to Panadura;

° Rs. 900 million for other operational expenses, including land acquisition, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 80,000 million, including the above.

12. Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises

Sector

We have recognized that the development of Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises -MSMEs- is most essential for a rapid transformation of the economy. Accordingly, in 2026, we have allocated Rs. 54,136 million for interest subsidies and refinancing for a number of schemes such as collateral-free loans, interest-free loans and low-interest loans. Through this, the State and private banking system has been able to grant loans amounting to Rs. 97,100 million owing to the surge that took place in that sector. Owing to the surge in that sector, we have been able to provide this entire allocation within this year.

Also, in 2027, Rs. 67,687 million has been allocated for interest subsidies and refinancing to banks. Through this, it is expected to provide Rs. 174 billion as concessionary loans to entrepreneurs through State and private banks. This includes the following loan schemes:

• Refinance and Interest Subsidy Loan Scheme for

Reviving the Micro, Small and Medium Enterprise Sector (Re-MSME Plus) ° The Government will provide an interest subsidy for the granting of a further Rs. 20,000 million in loans, in addition to the Rs. 30,000 million currently allocated to provide concessionary loans at an interest rate of 5 per cent for the capital and working capital requirements of Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises under the Refinance Loan Scheme, and it is also proposed to allocate Rs. 200 million for this purpose as the first phase. Steps will be taken to provide the additional provisions required. ° In order to make the fisheries sector eligible for this concessionary loan scheme, it is proposed to grant loans to enterprises engaged in the fisheries industry by considering only the business turnover in the definition of Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises.

• Schemes Implemented with Financial Assistance

of the Asian Development Bank ° In order to meet the existing demand for concessionary interest loan programmes, a further Rs. 30,000 million has been allocated, with the funding assistance of the Asian Development Bank, for granting loans for the capital and working capital requirements of Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises.

• Pledge Loan Scheme for Small and Medium-

Scale Paddy Mill Owners ° It is proposed to implement the Pledge Loan Scheme for small and medium-scale paddy mill owners for the purchase of paddy at the

Government guaranteed price as an interest-free loan scheme from 2027. It is expected to provide financial facilities of Rs. 20,000 million through this programme. In addition to the Rs. 350 million currently allocated, further allocations are proposed to bear the additional interest subsidy of this loan scheme.

• Loan Scheme for Uplifting Women-headed

Families ° A special programme is essential for uplifting women-headed family units. In particular, the living standards of such families living in rural areas are at a relatively low level. In order to strengthen such families economically, it is proposed to implement a group loan scheme of up to Rs. 1 million at a concessionary annual interest rate of 3 per cent through the Samurdhi Bank. For this, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 200 million as the first phase for the payment of interest subsidies. Steps will be taken to provide the additional provisions required. Through this, it is expected to provide loans amounting to Rs. 12,500 million.

• Programme for Empowering Rural Enterprises

° With the aim of strengthening enterprises in rural areas and empowering Aswesuma beneficiaries, it is expected to provide loans to entrepreneurs through the Samurdhi Bank up to a maximum of Rs. 5 million at a concessionary annual interest rate of 4 per cent. In order to qualify for this loan facility, it is mandatory for such entrepreneurs to provide employment opportunities to at least five Aswesuma beneficiaries. It is proposed to allocate Rs. 200 million for interest subsidies for this purpose. Through this, it is expected to provide loans amounting to Rs. 5,000 million.

• Concessionary Loans for Large-Scale Entrepreneurs in the Fisheries Industry

° It is proposed to provide a maximum loan amount of up to Rs. 500 million through banks at an annual interest rate of 5 per cent to large-scale fisheries industrialists with export potential, fish product suppliers, boat manufacturers and manufacturers of other fishing gear required for the fisheries industry, to obtain the modern technology and other inputs they require. It is proposed to provide Rs. 1,000 million as interest subsidies for this purpose. Through this, it is expected to provide loans amounting to Rs. 15,000 million.

• Home-Stay Loan Scheme

° It is proposed to provide concessionary loans of up to a maximum of Rs. 2 million at an annual interest rate of 3 per cent to entrepreneurs for businesses operating home-based tourist accommodation, to improve such facilities and to start new home-based tourist accommodation,

and it is proposed to allocate Rs. 100 million as the first phase for the payment of the necessary interest subsidies. Steps will be taken to provide the additional provisions required. It is expected to provide concessionary loans amounting to Rs. 7,500 million under this loan scheme.

The data and information required to access all these loan schemes have been included on the website of the Department of Development Finance of the Ministry of Finance, and a hotline of 1819 has also been introduced.

13. Port Development

Located at a highly strategic position on the East-West global sea route, the Port of Colombo holds 20th place among the ports of the world. Developing the port and its related services brings great importance to the country's economy. Our target is to increase its total shipping capacity to over 30 million Twenty-foot Equivalent Units (TEUs) by 2040, and to develop it as a re-export shipping and logistics hub.

• With financing from the Asian Development Bank

and the World Bank, a 3.2 kilometre breakwater will be constructed for the West Terminal II and the development required for handling 3.2 million containers will be carried out.

• The feasibility study currently being carried out for

the development of the Colombo North Port is expected to be completed in the first half of next year. It is expected to spend Rs. 300 million from the funds of the Ports Authority to expeditiously carry out the additional study on the Kelani River estuary, which is a decisive factor in the development of the North Port.

• Requests for Proposals – RFPs- for the establishment

of a Logistics Centre in the Port of Colombo through a public-private partnership will be called within the next two months.

• It has been planned to provide reliable and efficient

services by technically strengthening the Vessel Traffic Management System of the Port of Colombo. Provisions of Rs. 575 million have already been allocated to commence this project of Rs. 3,578 million, which is implemented with the assistance of the Government of Japan.

• Bunkering Capacity

With the number of vessels arriving at Sri Lankan ports increasing day by day, there is great potential to expand and strengthen bunkering capacity and to establish Sri Lanka as a competitive regional bunkering hub. Accordingly, with the support of the Ceylon Petroleum Corporation – CPC-, it is expected to increase capacity by 50,000 metric tons within the Port of Colombo under the participation of the private sector on a Build-Operate-Transfer -BOT- basis.

373 374

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

° The Ceylon Petroleum Corporation and the private sector will work jointly to develop the storage and pipeline systems required for bunkering services centred on the Port of Galle. ° There is potential to develop the Port of Trincomalee as a fuel storage hub as well as a strategic hub that can be connected to the energy supply network of the South Asian region. Steps will be taken to develop this jointly through State and international partnerships. ° Repair work on two of the five selected tanks in the Trincomalee Upper Tank Farm has already been completed. The construction of a new pipeline system from the tank farm to the port has been planned. The Ceylon Petroleum Corporation expects to spend Rs. 7,375 million for this purpose. ° It is expected to call for investment proposals -RFP- in 2027 to develop 29 of the 61 tanks owned by Trinco Petroleum Terminal (Private) Limited at the Port of Trincomalee, jointly established by the Ceylon Petroleum Corporation and Lanka IOC, under a public-private partnership.

We believe that once these development activities are implemented, Sri Lanka's maritime competitiveness will be significantly strengthened and the country will be established as a key energy hub in the global shipping and maritime industry, including offshore bunkering.

14. Development of Infrastructure in the

Aviation Sector

14.1 Development of International Airports

At present, although the passenger capacity of the first terminal of the Bandaranaike International Airport is around 6 million, a larger number of passengers is being handled. It has been forecast that a capacity of 16 million will be required by 2032. Accordingly, increasing the passenger capacity of the airport has already been delayed. Therefore, we consider its development a priority. Accordingly;

• Work to increase the capacity of the first terminal

from 6 million to 9 million has already commenced with an investment of Rs. 15,000 million by Airport and Aviation Services.

• Construction work on the delayed second terminal is

scheduled to commence very soon with the assistance of the Japan International Cooperation Agency –JIC-.

• Construction of a Domestic Air Terminal within the

airport premises will commence next year by Airport and Aviation Services (Sri Lanka) Limited –AASL- with an investment of Rs. 800 million, to enable international air passengers to travel more conveniently to domestic destinations.

• A feasibility study will be carried out for the

widening of the road in front of the Airport and the construction of a flyover to minimize traffic congestion at and around the Airport. It is proposed to allocate Rs. 200 million for this purpose.

• A new car park will be constructed by AASL at a

cost of Rs. 400 million to minimize vehicle congestion in and around the airport.

• A re-export hub will be developed within the Airport

on a Build-Operate-Transfer –BOT- basis to transform the Bandaranaike International Airport into an air cargo handling hub, with the participation of the private sector as well.

• The Ratmalana International Airport will be

developed for pilot training, and domestic, business and regional aviation. To ensure aviation safety, a project of Rs. 6,000 million to rehabilitate the existing runway and extend it from 1,400 metres to 1,771 metres will commence in 2027 with the funds of AASL.

• The Mattala International Airport, which was

constructed with a loan of US Dollars 190 million and commenced flight operations in 2013, has today recorded an accumulated loss of over Rs. 70,000 million. Moreover, this loss grows annually. Therefore, Expressions of Interest –EOI- have already been called and concluded for the development of the areas of airport operations, data centres, food and catering services, and aircraft services and maintenance jointly with international companies. Steps are being taken to call for Requests for Proposals –RFP- from the selected companies. We expect to select new investors for this within the next year.

14.2 Domestic Airports

• It is proposed to allocate a provision of Rs. 100

million for improving the facilities, including the passenger terminal, of the Sigiriya Domestic Airport for the promotion of tourism centred in Sigiriya.

• The necessary investments for improving the

facilities, including the passenger terminal, of the China Bay Airport will be made by AASL in 2027.

15. Industry, Trade and Investment

• Rs. 4,000 million has been allocated for the

development of the Valaichchenai Industrial Estate, carrying out feasibility studies and preparing preliminary plans for the Puliyankulam Industrial Estate, completing further development work on new industrial estates including Ingiriya, Dambulla, Raigama–Millaniya and Katunayake, and improving infrastructure facilities in existing industrial estates.

• Rs. 750 million has been allocated for activities

including holding "Sri Lanka Expo 2027" in January, and promoting export diversification, competitiveness and high-value exports by implementing the priorities identified under the National Export Development Plan –NEDP.

Encouraging Investment through the Capital Market

• At present, the number of persons actively trading in

connection with the Colombo Stock Exchange is only around 0.7 per cent of the population over 18 years of age. Therefore, it is proposed to implement a new programme named "A Share for Everyone" under the vision "Let's Know, Let's Learn, Let's Invest". With the aim of actively connecting students in schools who are studying for the Advanced Level in Grade 13 and are over 18 years of age with the stock market, it is proposed to introduce the project to provide a share portfolio subject to a maximum of Rs. 10,000, in accordance with a formal selection procedure.

the listing on the stock market of small and medium-scale enterprises -SME Listing Support Mechanism-, which makes a significant contribution to the economy and job creation.

Mineral Resources

We have still failed to properly integrate the mineral resources available in the country into our economy. There is great potential in our country to promote an

industrial system associated with mineral resources. The steps required to study the potential of these mineral resources and to integrate the benefits derived from mineral resources into the domestic economy have been presented in the National Policy on Mineral Resources. The Geological Survey and Mines Bureau will take the necessary steps to carry out preliminary feasibility studies of mineral resources, and steps will be taken to obtain specialized technical knowledge internationally for the exploration of mineral deposits.

• Exploration of the quantity of the graphite reserve in

the Kahatagaha Graphite Mine, which extends over an area of 102 acres, is scheduled to be carried out jointly with the Geological Survey and Mines Bureau. Once the size of the reserve has been identified, further steps are scheduled to be taken to identify a suitable investor for the graphite value addition project, including graphite mining and processing activities.

• Cabinet approval has been granted to allocate 58

grids containing mineral sands along the eastern coastal belt to Lanka Mineral Sands Limited. A Cabinet Appointed Negotiating Committee and a Project Committee have been appointed by the Cabinet of Ministers to select an investor for a value addition industry for the mineral sands mined by Lanka Mineral Sands Limited.

• Exploration is already being carried out on the silica

sand deposit extending over an area of 40 acres in the Nattandiya area. After identifying the potential of the relevant mineral resources, steps will be taken to identify a suitable investor in 2027.

• An area of 28 metric grid units has been identified in

relation to the Kotigambokka quartz deposit located in the Wellawaya area. Steps will be taken to identify a suitable investor in 2027 for the mining and value addition project of this quartz deposit.

16. Fisheries Industry

The fisheries industry is one of the most important industries, ensuring the country's food security, meeting a large percentage of the domestic protein requirement and uplifting the livelihoods of the fishing community. We expect to make the fisheries sector a more productive and sustainable sector through a modern approach based on modern technology, scientific management and the welfare of fishing communities. Accordingly, it has been planned to increase our export earnings of US Dollars 300 million to US Dollars 600 million by 2030. Rs. 6,200 million has been allocated for uplifting the fisheries industry, including the development of fishery harbours, strengthening new research relating to the fisheries industry at NARA, and promoting the inland fisheries industry.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million for the

development of the infrastructure required for the fisheries industry in the Northern Province, anchorages and the Pesalai Fishery Harbour.

problems, such as the absence of a formal waste disposal mechanism, inadequate cold storage facilities and inadequate sanitation facilities. It is proposed to provide Rs. 1,000 million as equity capital to resolve these issues. It is expected to manage this centre under a new business structure.

17. Agriculture

The main livelihoods of rural people are bound up with farming. Although agriculture accounts for 23.9 per cent of the labour force and around 39 per cent of land use, and more than Rs. 200 billion has been spent annually on public investment and subsidies over the past

few years, its contribution to the national economy is close to 8.4 per cent. These data themselves demonstrate the problem of farming life. It will not be possible to emerge from this crisis without subjecting this sector to a radical new transformation.

• Private sector investment will be encouraged to

increase large-scale agricultural production and its quality, improve the efficiency of agricultural value chains and expand markets.

• It is proposed to give paddy land owners the freedom

to use uncultivated or low-productivity paddy lands, after they have been identified through an accurate survey, for field crops or permanent crops. Rs. 1,550 million has been allocated to encourage cultivation on these selected lands.

• A collective "Yaya" production approach will be

introduced, instead of individual cultivation, for purchasing agricultural inputs in bulk, carrying out pest and disease control jointly, using water resources with maximum efficiency, facing price monopolies in the market and increasing productivity. Rs. 400 million has been allocated as an initial step.

• For rural agricultural development, Agrarian Service

Centres will be transformed into 'One-Stop Shops' through the establishment of digital infrastructure, the creation of machinery pools, the empowerment of farming communities and the establishment of a digital platform through which real-time information can be exchanged. Rs. 250 million has been allocated to launch this as a pilot project in five main agricultural districts.

• It is proposed to allocate an additional sum of Rs.

300 million in addition to Rs. 700 million already allocated for food security to enable co-operative societies which have already been selected to receive paddy dryers in 2026 and possess the necessary machinery capacity to purchase paddy from the Paddy Marketing Board and release it to the market. Furthermore, priority will be given to meeting the working capital requirements of these societies under an approved framework to facilitate the immediate commencement of these operations. This allocation is also intended to assist these co-operative societies in purchasing the machinery required for rice production.

• An Integrated Plant Nutrient Management System –

IPNM- is required to promote the integrated use of chemical fertilizer and organic fertilizer, increase crop productivity and strengthen soil quality and nutrient status. It is proposed to implement a four-year project for this purpose, and Rs. 47 million has been allocated for the year 2027 as an initial step.

• It is expected to provide the fertilizer subsidy of Rs.

30,000 per hectare for paddy cultivation, and Rs. 44,000 million has been allocated for this purpose. This subsidy of Rs. 30,000 per hectare of paddy cultivation will also be provided for other crops cultivated in paddy fields from the 2026/27 Maha season.
