17.2 Irrigation Sector

Developing and maintaining a strong irrigation system is essential to increase agricultural production, ensure food security, strengthen the livelihoods of rural people and efficiently manage water resources in the face of climate change. Through this Budget, Rs. 77,000 million has been allocated, providing adequate and prioritized financial provisions for irrigation development, to accelerate ongoing irrigation development activities and to commence new projects.

• Rs. 21,000 million has been estimated for the

rehabilitation of the cascaded tank systems damaged by "Ditwah" and the associated minor irrigation infrastructure and agricultural roads. These programmes commenced in 2026 with the Ditwah provisions, and the remaining provisions required will be provided in 2027.

• Although a feasibility study on the Jaffna Irrigation

Project was carried out in 2018 to improve the water security of the people of the Jaffna Peninsula, it has not been implemented to date. Rs. 75 million has been allocated for further feasibility studies, including on this project.

• Rs. 7,500 million has been allocated to accelerate the

work of the Lower Malwathu Oya Project, construction of which has already commenced, for the agricultural and social development of the Anuradhapura, Vavuniya and Mannar Districts.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million to meet

the water requirements of the Batticaloa and Ampara Districts, minimize flood risk and commence the Mundeni Aru River Basin Development Project.

• The water potential of the Hambantota Industrial

Zone and its associated areas will be around 115 million cubic metres by 2040. There is an additional water requirement of approximately 115 million cubic metres –MCM- per year for domestic and industrial needs. To find solutions to this requirement, water sources including the Rakwana Ganga, the additional effective water capacity of the Samanalawewa Reservoir and the Uma Oya water transfer to the Kirindi Oya Basin have been identified, and the necessary provisions have been allocated to carry out an integrated water balance and pre-feasibility study on them.

• Rs. 5,000 million has been allocated for irrigation

projects such as the implementation of the Wayamba Canal Project to divert surplus water from the Weemedilla Reservoir to the Mee Oya Basin in the Kurunegala District, the modernization of the over 50-year-old Rajanganaya lift irrigation scheme, the completion of work on the halted feeder canal from the Siyambalangamuwa Oya to the Mahagalgamuwa Oya and the mitigation of floods in Gampaha Town.

• Rs. 19,500 million has been allocated for the

Mahaweli Water Security and Investment Programme, including the North Central Province Greater Canal Project.

• It is expected to carry out feasibility studies to

recommence the Kumbukkan Oya Reservoir Project aimed at meeting the water requirements of the Monaragala District and the Heda Oya Reservoir Development Project aimed at improving the water supply of the Ampara District. It is proposed to allocate an additional Rs. 200 million for the preliminary work of such irrigation projects.

Rehabilitation of Tanks, Anicuts and Canal Bunds

° Minor irrigation schemes are intertwined with life, supplying the water required for the cultivation of paddy and other field crops, maintaining the groundwater table at saturation, facilitating the inland fisheries industry, as well as meeting the water consumption needs of the rural community. 18. Plantation Sector

It is expected to increase the export earnings of US Dollars 3 billion currently generated by the plantation sector to US Dollars 5 billion by 2030. For this, Rs. 13,000 million has been allocated for the development of the plantation sector in 2027.

• Rs. 730 million has been allocated to implement the

programme to establish 500 tea villages covering the Southern, Sabaragamuwa, North Western, Western, Uva and Central Provinces through an integrated approach, aimed at increasing the overall productivity of the smallholder tea sector, which contributes around 70 per cent of tea production.

• In addition to the Rs. 500 million allocated for the

rehabilitation of abandoned mature rubber plantations that are not tapped, provisions have been allocated for increasing rubber productivity in non-traditional areas by introducing suitable water management technologies, carrying out research to identify the commercial uses of rubber wood, and improving laboratory infrastructure related to the rubber industry.

• Rs. 2,500 million has been allocated for the Coconut

Rehabilitation Project to increase the productivity of low-productive coconut estates.

• Rs. 100 million has been allocated to implement a

• Rs. 3,000 million has been allocated for infrastructure development, including construction of field rest rooms, improvement of toilet facilities and rehabilitation of Praja Shakthi centres, with the aim of increasing the productivity of the plantation workforce.

• The 2027 Budget has allocated Rs. 820 million to

provide subsidies to small-scale tea estate owners for replanting of tea and the infilling of vacancies on their lands.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million to provide

a subsidy of Rs. 4,000 per bag of fertilizer obtained by small-scale tea holders, in order to ensure continuous application of fertilizer in tea cultivation.

• Rs. 650 million has been allocated to encourage

rubber growers, provide concessions for new planting and replanting, and promote rubber cultivation in non-traditional areas.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million to provide

a fertilizer subsidy of Rs. 4,000 per bag, with the aim of ensuring the continued application of fertilizer in rubber plantations.

• Provisions have been allocated to continue in 2027

the incentive allowance of Rs. 200 per day provided to estate workers last year.

• The lease agreements of Regional Plantation

Companies are set to expire in the year 2045. The Cabinet has appointed a committee of officials to review these agreements, with the objective of safeguarding the rights of both estate owners and employees, while also analysing the productivity of the respective estates.

20. Social Protection and Empowerment

The achievements of Sri Lanka in social development and social protection are at a high level compared to many developing countries. However, many new challenges are emerging within the country due to significant changes in household population composition and social dynamics. In particular, the decrease in the number of household members, the increase in the elderly population and the low level of female labour force participation are prominent among these.

• Rs. 230 billion has been allocated in 2027 for social protection allowances, including Aswesuma, to minimize economic hardships and ensure the social protection of the poor and extremely poor groups by continuing to provide them with Aswesuma benefits.

• The monthly allowance provided to kidney patients will be increased by Rs. 2,500, from Rs. 10,000 to Rs. 12,500.

• The monthly allowance provided to persons with disabilities will be increased by Rs. 2,500, from Rs. 10,000 to Rs. 12,500.

• Rs. 25,000 million has been allocated to implement the integrated rural development programme Island-wide under the Praja Shakthi National Movement, giving priority attention to the eradication of poverty.

• An empowerment pilot project was implemented with the assistance of projects funded by the Asian Development Bank and the World Bank, and Rs. 4,500 million has been allocated for the programme to systematically empower eligible families with potential.

• Rs. 182 million has been allocated for the holistic personality development of children through a family-centred approach, with the aim of ensuring the protection, health, education and mental well-being of vulnerable children.

• Rs. 102 million has been allocated for an alternative care structure focused on vulnerable children and children who have lost their parents due to disasters.

• A Disability Inclusion Focal Point will be established in every Ministry, department and major public institution for the community of persons with disabilities.

• A sum of Rs. 150 million has been allocated under the National Secretariat for Persons with Disabilities to provide technical equipment, medical aid, or other facilities to individuals with disabilities who are otherwise unable to obtain them, thereby facilitating their daily lives. It is proposed that this programme be implemented on a broader scale through coordination with the President’s Fund and other relevant institutions.

• The establishment of accessible digital public services will be made mandatory, that is, taking steps to design all public digital services in accordance with the W3C accessibility specifications.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million for a programme to be implemented by 'Clean Sri Lanka' to ensure physical accessibility within government institutions giving effect to a Supreme Court order and addressing the mobility needs of the elderly and persons with disabilities in the

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

country. Furthermore, it is ensured that provisions will be made available to every institution that requests such support.

• Rs. 100 million has been allocated to develop the “Pannala Welcome Village” elders' home under the National Secretariat for Elders as a model elderly care centre.

• Rs. 75 million has been allocated for the construction of four new wards and a wastewater management system for homeless and destitute persons at the Rediyagama Kalana Detention Home.

• Day care centres will be constructed for children with disabilities, and Rs. 200 million has been allocated to provide transport facilities for children with disabilities.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 100 million to extend State patronage for meeting the basic needs of care centres for persons with disabilities run by non-governmental organizations.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 500 million to provide facilities for high-capacity children's homes.

Sri Lankans Overseas

• We must take a new approach to connect the knowledge and abilities of Sri Lankans living abroad and their children, as well as their bond to the motherland, to the future development journey of the country. Accordingly, we propose to introduce a "Global Sri Lankan Heritage Citizen" programme for the second and third generations of the overseas community of Sri Lankan origin. Accordingly, we propose to grant dual citizenship, free of any charge, to the spouse and children of an applicant for dual citizenship, as well as to third-generation children.

• A highly concessionary housing loan scheme of up to Rs. 5 million will be implemented to meet the housing needs of migrant workers. Under this scheme, steps will be taken to provide an interest subsidy of 10 per cent for up to Rs. 3 million and an interest subsidy of 5 per cent for the remaining Rs. 2 million.

• It is proposed that, for the first Rs. 3 million, a contribution of 6 per cent be borne by the Government and a contribution of 4 per cent by the Sri Lanka Bureau of Foreign Employment, and for the remaining Rs. 2 million, a contribution of 3 per cent be borne by the Government and a contribution of 2 per cent by the Sri Lanka Bureau of Foreign Employment.

It is proposed to allocate a sum of Rs. 1,000 million in 2027 as the Government contribution for the implementation of this interest subsidy scheme.

21. Disaster Management

• The installation of a modern Doppler Radar System for highly accurate weather forecasting, received as a JICA grant, will commence in 2027.

• Rs. 1,000 million has been allocated for the rehabilitation of safe centres to ensure the safety of people during disasters.

• Rs. 500 million has been allocated to provide disaster response equipment essential for strengthening early warning.

• Rs. 670 million has been allocated to establish an Island-wide digital radio communication system.

• Rs. 500 million has been allocated for the rehabilitation of 77 existing coastal early warning towers, the construction of 120 new land-based towers covering flood, landslide and tsunami-prone areas in 25 districts, and the establishment of a multi-hazard early warning communication system.

• Including the above proposals, Rs. 2,900 million has been allocated for 13 new disaster management projects.

• A Dam Safety Act will be introduced to provide the legal safeguards necessary to ensure the safety of major dam systems.

22. Health Services

Capital expenditure of Rs. 131 billion has been allocated for maintaining and improving the public health service so that every citizen is able to obtain the quality health services they require through all medical systems administered by the Central Government and the provincial councils.

• A large number of vacancies existed due to the non-recruitment of health staff over a long period. In order to deliver a quality health service, the required human resources have been recruited, namely 1,797 doctors, 303 indigenous medical practitioners, 64 dental surgeons, 3,441 nurses, 952 public health midwives and 3,415 support staff in various areas.

• The pilot project of 39 "Arogya Wellness Centres", aimed at providing patient-centred health services close to between 5,000 - 10,000 of the population, is being successfully implemented, and it is expected to construct 1,000 centres during the period 2027 – 2029 with an investment of Rs. 17,440 million jointly with the provincial councils. Rs. 3,600 million has been allocated for the year 2027 for this purpose.

transfer to the Central Government the District General Hospitals of Mannar, Mullaitivu, Vavuniya, Kilinochchi, Gampaha and Avissawella, which are administered by the provincial councils, out of the 20 District General Hospitals.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 300 million for the construction of an Accident and Emergency Treatment Unit with modern facilities at the Thambuttegama Base Hospital.

• Provisions of Rs. 65,587 million are required to fully meet, within three years, the requirement for high-end biomedical equipment identified through a formal study, including MRI, CT and Cath Lab, which are necessary to enhance diagnostic and treatment services in all hospitals. For this, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million in addition to the provisions of Rs. 15,500 million allocated for the year 2027.

• There are 400 ambulances in the health service that are more than 20 years old, of which around 50 per cent are more than 25 years old. In order to ensure a better quality and more efficient service to the people, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 3,000 million in 2027 to add new ambulances to the system in place of the old ambulances. In addition, 25 ambulances through Asian Development Bank loan assistance and 20 new ambulances as a grant from Japan, coordinated by the Asian Development Bank, will be added within the next year.

• 100 new ambulances will be added in 2027 under Indian assistance of Rs. 1,725 million to expand the 1990 Suwa Seriya service, which consists of 319 ambulances.

• An organ transplant centre, including for the liver, at a cost of Rs. 3,155 million, will be established within the National Hospital of Colombo with the financial contribution of local and foreign institutions and organizations. Rs. 50 million has been allocated in 2027 for the initial activities.

• Rs. 400 million has been allocated to implement widely within the country the biological Wolbachia dengue vector control method with the technical contribution of the World Mosquito Programme - WMP - and the co-financing contribution of the Government, to control the dengue epidemic.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 100 million to provide spectacles free of charge to school children to minimize disruptions to their education caused by visual impairments.

• In addition to the development projects currently being implemented in hospitals providing secondary and tertiary care services, Rs. 1,245 million has been allocated to commence a number of new projects, including official quarters, at the Badulla and Batticaloa Teaching Hospitals, the

Matale and Nawalapitiya District General Hospitals and the Puttalam Base Hospital.

• Rs. 510 million has been allocated to prepare, in 2027, feasibility studies and preliminary plans relating to the identified development projects in hospitals, including the Matara District General Hospital, its relocation to the Kotawila area in Kamburugamuwa, the Kurunegala, Kalubowila and Kuliyapitiya Teaching Hospitals, the Trincomalee Siddha Teaching Hospital, the Moneragala and Polonnaruwa District General Hospitals, the National Institute of Infectious Diseases and the Gampola Base Hospital, as well as to providing accommodation facilities for hospital staff and establishing regional medical supplies stores as required.

• It is proposed to establish a centre within the Faculty of Medicine, Ragama to train doctors in Robotic Surgery. It is expected to provide training for the work of this centre in collaboration with foreign universities. It is proposed to allocate Rs. 250 million for this purpose.

• The “Rabies-Free Sri Lanka by 2030” programme is being implemented with the goal of eradicating canine rabies in the country by 2030, primarily through the administration of rabies vaccines to dogs and the streamlining of canine sterilization programmes. It is proposed that this initiative be carried out as a collaborative effort, integrating the canine rabies control programme managed under the Ministry of Health and Mass Media with technical support from the Department of Animal Production and Health, while also utilizing the infrastructure available within local government institutions. We intend to ensure that the allocations already provided to the institutions dealing with this matter are properly coordinated and implemented.

23. Tourism Sector

The diversity of Sri Lanka's tourist attractions is the most important and strongest potential of the country's tourism industry. Tourism is one of the main sectors that activates the country's economy, earns foreign exchange and generates employment. The earnings from tourist arrivals by September this year amounted to US Dollars 2,245 million. Therefore, attention has been paid to uplifting the rural economy and generating employment through the tourism industry.

• Rs. 300 million will be deployed from the funds of the Sri Lanka Tourism Development Authority to review the progress of the Southern Mist Triangle project, which is implemented centred on the Sinharaja evergreen rainforest covering the Galle, Matara and Ratnapura Districts in the Southern and Sabaragamuwa Provinces, and to further strengthen it.

• Rs. 35 million has been allocated, in addition to the capital contribution of Rs.

389 390

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

• 133 million provided by the Sri Lanka Tourism Development Authority - SLTDA - , to establish a centralized online e-ticketing system covering the main tourist attractions of Sri Lanka.

• With the financial assistance of the World Bank, at a cost of US Dollars 77 million, the historic and ancient buildings located around Colombo Fort will be restored in a manner that protects their historical value and developed as a tourism zone.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 500 million to develop the Indian-assisted trilingual school premises in Polonnaruwa into a hotel school, including trilingual teaching, and to develop the Pinnawala Hotel School.

• The historic Dutch forts located in the areas of Jaffna, Mannar, Kalpitiya and Batticaloa will be promoted as tourism development hubs under public-private partnerships - PPP -, while ensuring the antiquarian value and conservation of the historic heritage. It is expected to deploy Rs. 240 million for this purpose from the underutilized funds of the Sri Lanka Tourism Development Authority - SLTDA.

• It is expected to deploy Rs. 500 million from the funds of the Sri Lanka Tourism Promotion Bureau - SLTPB - for a concessionary loan scheme for tourism service providers to obtain the necessary equipment, including boats, to promote marine tourism.

• A cruise tourism passenger terminal will be established in the Port of Colombo. Financing of Rs. 240 million will be provided from the funds of the Sri Lanka Tourism Development Authority - SLTDA - to modernize passenger terminal service facilities in the areas of Galle, Tangalle and Trincomalee and to expand the services provided to tourists.

• It is expected to further strengthen the Tourist Police service to promote Sri Lanka as a safe tourist destination. It is proposed to provide a prompt and efficient security service to tourists by providing specialized training, deploying officers with multilingual capabilities, establishing special patrol units around major tourist sites, and introducing a 24-hour tourist emergency hotline service and a mobile application.

24. Environmental Management

It is our duty to safeguard environmental balance for sustainable economic development. It is one of our fundamental responsibilities to secure the natural resources of the country for future generations by facing the challenges of climate change, protecting biodiversity and moving towards a green economy approach.

• Our Government, which has understood the importance of the wildlife sector, has recruited

313 wildlife guards and 3,451 electric fence maintenance assistants to strengthen the services of the Department of Wildlife Conservation for its protection and management. Provisions have been allocated for the recruitment of 114 wildlife rangers. Vehicles and equipment, including 16 double cabs, 84 single cabs, 181 motorcycles and nine tractors have been added in 2026 for field operations to deliver wildlife services efficiently.

• With the aim of minimizing the human-elephant conflict and protecting human lives and wild elephants, the Handapanagala, Koholankala and Wetahira Kanda elephant corridors were released in June, 2026, and it is expected to declare the elephant corridor located between the Nachchaduwa Wewa and Maha Kanadarawa Wewa as a wildlife reserve.

• Rs. 250 million has been allocated for the construction of new electric fences and the rehabilitation of existing electric fences. In addition, it is expected to allocate provisions of Rs. 200 million for this purpose from the Wildlife Fund as well.

• A pilot project has been launched using artificial intelligence - AI - and thermal cameras to minimize accidents caused by wild elephants colliding with trains. Accordingly, it has been possible to reduce the number of such deaths this year. Based on its results, it is expected to implement this project nationally.

• A land area of three acres has been identified within the Nuwaragam Forest Reserve in the Anuradhapura District to construct a hospital capable of treating wild animals for animal welfare. It is expected to allocate Rs. 700 million from the Wildlife Fund for the establishment of this hospital.

• Stranded baby elephants from all areas are currently rehabilitated at the Udawalawe Elephant Transit Home, and their lives are at risk due to the need to transport the baby elephants over very long distances. Therefore, it is expected to provide estimated provisions of Rs. 5,000 million in several phases from the Wildlife Fund for the construction of a new elephant transit home on a land area of 15 acres in the Polonnaruwa District.

• Rs. 50 million has been allocated from the Wildlife Fund for the initial work of constructing a centre for the treatment and rehabilitation of injured marine animals such as turtles and dolphins on a land area of two acres in the Dodanduwa area.

We expect to further strengthen the legal framework to protect the wild elephant resource, which is a living heritage of Sri Lanka, and to prevent cruelty inflicted on them.

• It is proposed to increase the compensation paid for damage to a house due to the human-elephant conflict from Rs. 200,000 to Rs. 1 million, the compensation paid for a person who dies or

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

• It is proposed to amend the Fauna and Flora Protection Ordinance to increase, in addition to the existing term of imprisonment, the fine for killing, hunting, shooting, wounding or capturing a wild elephant from the current fine of Rs. 150,000 to Rs. 500,000 to Rs. 500,000 to Rs. 1 million. It is proposed to amend the Increase of Fines Act, No. 12 of 2005 to increase the fine for illegally keeping an elephant in possession from the current Rs. 150,000 to Rs. 250,000 to Rs. 500,000 to Rs. 1 million.

• Animals may face a water shortage due to the drying up of tanks within wildlife reserves. To face this situation, funds will be allocated through the Wildlife Conservation Fund in 2027 for the rehabilitation of the tanks currently identified by the Department of Wildlife Conservation as requiring rehabilitation. Further, an additional Rs. 90 million has been allocated for this purpose from the 2027 Budget.

• In addition to the above provisions, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million as advance preparedness to meet the requirements that may arise from the El Niño risk expected in the future.

• Provisions have already been allocated to obtain vessels and machinery worth Rs. 3,380 million under Japanese cooperation to strengthen the capacity to respond promptly to marine and coastal pollution caused by oil spills, in order to protect the environment of the coastal belt.

• Rs. 800 million has been allocated for 10 new sustainable environmental management projects, including the protection and stability of the eastern coastal belt, coastal protection in the Karukupane area in the Puttalam District, and the establishment of an ambient air quality monitoring network.

• The protection of the central highlands, which are under severe threat, is the protection of the future of our nation. For this, it is proposed to establish an Upper Watershed Area Management Authority to establish an integrated watershed management approach and for restoration activities. It is expected to present the relevant Act to Parliament very soon. Discussions have already commenced to obtain loan assistance of US Dollars 100 million for the functions to be assigned to this Authority.

7. Transport

It is the expectation of our Government to usher in a new era in the transport sector to ensure an efficient, comfortable public transport service. Accordingly, a record Rs. 95,000 million will be deployed for

modernizing railway and passenger bus services, strengthening connectivity between transport networks and promoting sustainable public transport.

• It is expected to extend the Metro Bus service introduced around Colombo this year to the urban areas of Kandy, Galle and Jaffna as well.

• The Government has invested in Metro Buses up to now. More than 120 buses are already in operation. A further 50 buses are scheduled to be added to that company in 2027. In addition, we have also planned to introduce electric buses. One hundred buses are scheduled to be received for this purpose as a donation from the Government of China. Activities such as the construction of the depots required for these buses, the provision of the necessary charging facilities and the provision of training will commence next year, and provisions of Rs. 4,500 million have been allocated for this purpose.

• To encourage the private sector to deploy Metro Buses as well, existing route permit holders will be encouraged to add a modern low-floor bus to operation in place of their existing buses.

• Steps have already been taken to import 600 air-conditioned buses to provide a comfortable and safe service to long-distance passengers.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million to purchase 50 small buses with 22 seats required for rural areas with narrow hill roads.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 4,000 million to obtain 200 small buses with 33 seats required for passenger services in rural areas.

• Rs. 200 million has been allocated for the Digital Transformation and Integrated Management Systems Development Project of the Sri Lanka Transport Board.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 600 million to establish the Department of Motor Traffic on the Werahera premises in order to provide a more efficient service.

• Rs. 700 million has been allocated to establish a modern digital system for the process of vehicle registration and transfer of ownership, with the aim of providing efficient and transparent services to the public.

• Rs. 1,500 million has been allocated to establish a new digital Driver Demerit Points System and a road safety enforcement system, integrating the Department of Motor Traffic and the Sri Lanka Police, to minimize road accidents and for road safety.

• Rs. 5,000 million has been allocated to purchase five new trains for the railway service.

• Provisions of Rs. 2,000 million have been allocated in 2027 for the programme to repair railway stations that have not been properly maintained for a long time.

• Train operations between Colombo and Peradeniya have been resumed by deploying the resources of the Department of Railways with maximum efficiency to restore the up-country railway line damaged by the Ditwah disaster.

• Under the Government of India loan assistance programme, the work required to rehabilitate the railway section from Rambukkana to Peradeniya, the up-country railway line from Peradeniya to Gampola, and the Matale railway line in a safer and more stable manner, in accordance with modern engineering standards, has commenced. • Under the Cyclone Ditwah damage rehabilitation programme of the Government of China, rehabilitation work on nine bridges located in the Gampaha, Kegalle, Ampara, Kurunegala, Polonnaruwa and Batticaloa Districts has been prepared through a grant for the reconstruction of 14 destroyed bridges.

26. Drinking Water Supply

Rs. 77,000 million has been allocated for the uninterrupted supply of clean drinking water to the people, the expansion of sanitation facilities, and the supply of adequate quantities of water for economic activities, including industries. Further, the invitation of private investment is being considered.

• Rs. 5,190 million has been allocated to commence 13 new projects, including in Jaffna, Mannar, Mullaitivu, Vavuniya, Nagadeepa, Valaichchenai and Kotte, to meet drinking water requirements.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,800 million for the year 2027 for the commencement of the Kundasale–Haragama Water Supply Project with a cost of Rs. 34,368 million.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million for the commencement of the Eheliyagoda Water Supply Project with a cost of Rs. 4,775 million.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,800 million for the commencement of the Greater Kilinochchi Water Supply Project with a cost of Rs. 41,140

million, covering the Karachchi, Kandavalai, Poonakary and Pachchilaipalli Divisional Secretary’s Divisions in the Kilinochchi District.

• Rs. 3,000 million has been allocated for the commencement of the Greater Galle City Water Supply Project, with a cost of Rs. 37,501 million.

• Rs. 700 million has been allocated for the commencement of the Haldummulla–Beragala Water Supply Project with a cost of Rs. 3,262 million.

• Rs. 300 million has been allocated in 2027 for the commencement of the Yatimahana–Ma Oya Water Supply Project, with a cost of Rs. 22,158 million.

• Provisions will continuously be provided to resolve step by step the problems relating to drinking water in the Jaffna and the Vanni Districts, such as seawater intrusion, high hardness, high mineral salts and bacterial contamination. It is proposed to allocate Rs. 1,000 million for this purpose.

• Rs. 12,000 million has been allocated to implement projects, including improving the capacity of existing drinking water projects, expanding distribution and constructing sanitation facilities.

• Rs. 250 million has been allocated for feasibility studies to meet the water requirements for industrial and commercial activities in the areas of Kuliyapitiya, Pannala, Bingiriya, Udubaddawa, Trincomalee and Gampola–Jayamalapura.

• Rs. 2,000 million has been allocated for improving community water supply schemes across the Island.

• Rs. 1,740 million has been allocated to implement the Ella, Moratuwa and Ekala wastewater management projects with the aim of improving sanitation facilities and managing wastewater in a formal manner.

27. Housing and Urban Development

A secure house and a healthy urban environment are the most fundamental basis for the progress of a nation and its human resources. Our expectation is to uplift the living standards of the people by planning economic, social and physical development in an integrated manner, while protecting the natural and built environment.

• It is expected to construct 70,000 houses under the "A Place of One's Own – A Beautiful Life" programme to resolve the housing problem of low-income earners. Under this programme, which has already commenced, it is expected to construct 10,000 new houses in 2027. The housing assistance provided per house under this programme will be increased from Rs. 1 million to Rs. 1.5 million. It is proposed to increase the provision currently allocated for this purpose to Rs. 15,000 million.

• Thirteen thousand families displaced due to the armed conflict that prevailed in the North and the East have still not been resettled. • It is proposed to allocate a provision of Rs. 1,000 million for providing houses to those in areas such as Abaranchimulla, Serakkuliya, Mangalapura, Kandasamy Nagar and Nithya Nagar in Vavuniya, Pediyafarm, Polonnaruwa, Mullaitivu, Tissamaharama, Divulapitiya, Dimbulagala and Hennanigala-South who were affected by the Ditwah disaster but did not become entitled to a house due to the existing criteria.

A sum of Rs. 2,700 million has been allocated for this purpose.

• In addition, it is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million for the year 2027 for 19 towns, namely, Trincomalee, Kantale, Kalmunai, Dehiwala, Kalutara, Panadura, Matugama, Maharagama, Ratnapura, Kegalle, Bandarawela, Ella, Galle, Akuressa, Kurunegala, Puttalam, Nattandiya, Kiribathgoda and Nuwara Eliya over and above the provisions already made for development activities through project estimates totaling Rs. 65,000 million.

• The need to efficiently maintain drainage systems, carry out emergency operations during floods and systematically maintain small canals and drainage systems in order to minimize flood risk in urban low-lying areas is increasing. For this, steps will be taken to obtain multipurpose machinery worth Rs. 1,250 million for the Sri Lanka Land Development Corporation, and it is proposed to provide Rs. 625 million of this as Government equity capital.

• It is expected to encourage the Government, the private sector, banks and finance companies to jointly provide housing to resolve the housing problem of families working in the public, semi-government and private sectors and those engaged in their own businesses. Under this programme, we expect to provide the facilities required to construct 10,000 apartment units in major cities over the next five years.

Urban Flood Control

• Urban flooding is caused by the failure to properly maintain the main canals in the City of Colombo and its suburbs in a timely manner, as well as the insufficient capacity of the canals that carry rainwater. In addition to the flood mitigation projects already underway in Kolonnawa and Oliyumulla areasassociated with the City of Colombo, several other key initiatives are slated to commence in 2027. These include the Mudun Ela Project - aimed at flood prevention in the Wattala, Kelaniya, and Biyagama areas -, a project to upgrade the stormwater drainage system in Piliyandala and Kesbewa, as well as the rehabilitation projects for the St. Sebastian Canal and the Kirulapona Canal, both designed to control flooding in the North Colombo region.

• Additionally, there are plans to develop a section of the Hamilton Canal. Furthermore, projects to improve the Bindunu Ela - canal - system in Wadduwa and Molligoda - Kalutara District - as well as rainwater drainage systems in the Gampaha and Ratnapura Districts, and in Dikwella, Beruwala and Rathgama are set to commence in 2027.

• Rs. 3,200 million has been allocated for projects aimed at improving urban flood control and environmental systems, including the restoration of identified canals in Colombo and its suburban

397 398

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

cities and the improvement of the stormwater drainage system adjacent to the Piliyandala–Kesbewa by-road.

• Furthermore, the ongoing Malwathu Oya project will provide a solution to the flooding issues in the Mannar District. Additionally, preliminary work on the Mundeni Aru Project, which directly addresses flooding concerns in the Ampara and Batticaloa Districts, is expected to commence next year. Accordingly, an allocation of Rs. 7,700 million has been set aside for rainwater management.

• Rs. 900 million has been allocated to carry out a scientific study on the sustainable development of the Beira Lake and the surrounding land use and prepare a development plan, and in the meantime, to implement the essential development activities already identified.

• I emphasized in the last Budget the need for a sustainable solution for urban flood control. Rs. 250 million was allocated to prepare a short-term, medium-term and long-term plan for this purpose. That plan is currently being prepared.

28. Public Security

• Rs. 9,500 million has been allocated for the digitalization of the duties of the Sri Lanka Police, the complete modernization of the National Police Academy, the development of the Police Hospital in Narahenpita, the development of the Police Training School in Attidiya, the construction of the Police Sports Complex in Mirihana and the upgrading of facilities and infrastructure at the Special Task Force camps in Gonahena, Kurunegala, Arantalawa, Kegalle and Kirindiwela.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 200 million for feasibility studies and the preparation of plans to construct a new administrative building to house in a single building the various divisions of the Sri Lanka Police Department, which are currently operating in buildings obtained on a rental basis at various locations in the City of Colombo, with the aim of providing a more efficient service to the public.

• Rs. 500 million has been allocated for feasibility studies and the preparation of plans to construct a 15-storey building with the modern facilities required for investigating evolving crimes such as cybercrime and the suppression of financial fraud, for the Criminal Investigation Department, which is operating in a severely dilapidated building in Colombo Fort.

• It is proposed to provide police officers in June, 2027 with a new uniform suited to the climatic conditions of our country and equipped with modern technological devices.

• Rehabilitating persons addicted to dangerous drugs and reintegrating them into society is a long-term and important objective of the "Ratama Ekata" National Operation. For this, Rs. 180 million has been allocated for the development of infrastructure at the Senapura and Kandakadu rehabilitation centres and the full development of counselling and treatment services and vocational training facilities.

• Rs. 6,200 million has been allocated for activities including ensuring border security in line with national and international policies and standards on immigration and emigration, developing the Wattala Temporary Detention Centre with facilities conforming to international standards for foreigners temporarily detained due to visa crises and complications, establishing a new office complex in Trincomalee to enhance regional operations, and digitalizing immigration and emigration activities.

• Legislative work has already commenced to grant permanent residency to investors based on the size of the investment and other relevant criteria.

29. Judiciary

The most fundamental basis for the realization of democracy and the rule of law in a country is the ability to administer justice fairly and without delay. The judicial and prison sector has come under severe pressure due to the long-standing shortage of infrastructure, technological underdevelopment and systemic delays. It is proposed to provide solutions by upgrading the infrastructure of the judicial sector, integrating modern technology and carrying out systemic reforms.

• Rs. 5,115 million has been allocated for the expansion and development of facilities in the existing courts in Jaffna, Batticaloa, Trincomalee, Mannar, Nuwara Eliya, Kegalle, Gampaha, Kalutara, Kandy and Colombo.

• Rs. 60 million has been allocated for the complete digitalization of the activities of the Government Analyst's Department.

• The Supreme Court e-filing portal, the capacity for paperless hearings, integrated case management and online payments - e-CMS - have already been implemented as components of the programme to obtain the support of digitalization for the efficiency of the judicial system. The implementation of this programme within the Supreme Court has now been completed, and the process for its implementation in other courts has commenced.

Severe congestion has arisen within the prison system of our country. Exceeding the capacity to hold around 16,500 persons in 22 prisons and 14 work camps, nearly 36,500 inmates, around twice that number, are currently being held. Officers as well as inmates have lost their lives due to unfortunate incidents that occurred within prisons as a result of this congestion and the drug menace.

• Due to the severe congestion in the Colombo prison complex, including the Welikada Prison, the Magazine Prison and the Colombo Remand Prison, it is proposed to allocate a sum of Rs. 300 million in this Budget for the commencement of the relocation of these prison complexes to the Millewa area.

• In line with the Galle Heritage City Development Plan and tourism zone promotion activities, it is proposed to allocate a sum of Rs. 500 million in this Budget for the commencement of the relocation of the current Galle Prison from its location to the Boossa area.

• Rs. 1,000 million has been allocated for the expansion of the Wariyapola and Watareka Prisons, where development work has already commenced, to increase their holding capacity.

• Rs. 500 million has been allocated for the relocation of the Matara Prison to the Kotawilawatta area in Matara.

30. National Security

• Provisions of Rs. 250 million have been allocated for the establishment of a Defence Development Research Centre, affiliated to the Ministry of Defence, for research carried out by the security forces.

• There are teams in the security forces recruited for construction work. It is proposed to provide a special allowance to these personnel when they are deployed in various development programmes of the Government.

• As a tribute to the parents of war heroes who joined the armed forces before 2009, it is proposed that the Ministry of Defence expeditiously prepare a programme to increase the monthly allowance of Rs. 750 currently paid to the mother and father and to implement it from July, 2027.

• An institution to provide consultancy services for public sector construction will be established by attaching professionals, including engineers, serving in the Tri-forces. This institution will function as a consultancy institution in the planning, monitoring and supervision of Government projects.

• Steps will be taken to modernize the digital and cybersecurity systems of the Ministry of Defence, establish a digital forensic laboratory and develop border security technological facilities. It is also expected to improve the communication and technological facilities of the Sri Lanka Army, and to provide new patrol craft, tug boats and modern radar systems to strengthen the maritime security and surveillance capabilities of the Sri Lanka Navy.

31. Cultural and National Heritage

Our national identity has been established through all ethnicities and religions living in harmony as one family. It is one of our foremost responsibilities to formally conserve that heritage in order to bequeath it to future generations.

For this, steps will be taken to digitalize and conserve sacred places, historical artefacts and cultural identities through digital technological innovations such as 3D laser scanning used internationally as well as locally. This will make it possible to permanently maintain accurate three-dimensional models of the national heritage even in the face of natural disasters or weather impacts.

Rs. 7,000 million has been allocated in this Budget for the promotion and development of artistic activities associated with various ethnicities and religions.

• Anuradhapura is a sacred area of national and international importance in which many unique historical, archaeological, religious and cultural heritages of Sri Lanka are concentrated. It represents the historical heritage of Buddhist civilization. Therefore, we expect to implement a number of development activities, including improving the facilities required by devotees, road rehabilitation and waste management, while safeguarding its archaeological and religious values for future generations. An advisory council, co-chaired by the Acting Chief Prelate of the Atamasthana, has already been established to monitor and guide this programme. For this purpose, Rs. 1,076 million has already been allocated by the Central Cultural Fund for the year 2027. The Urban Development Authority has prepared an estimate of Rs. 8,200 million, and it is proposed to allocate Rs. 500 million for the year 2027.

• Funds have been allocated to the Ministry of Housing and Water Supply to implement the “Yathiwara” housing programme for parents, which was initiated in 2026, during the year 2027.

• The Government has provided provisions of Rs. 722 million for the rehabilitation of 1,328 places of religious worship damaged by the Ditwah disaster, comprising 758 Buddhist temples, 272 Hindu places of worship, 199 Muslim places of worship and 99 Christian places of worship. In addition, among the 1,328 destroyed places of religious worship, Rs. 40 million from the Buddha Sasana Fund for 30 Buddhist temples, and Rs. 523 million from the Central Cultural Fund for archaeological sites, including places of religious worship, are being utilized for rehabilitation, and a temple is also being rehabilitated through the Anagarika Dharmapala Trust with funds donated by the Maha Bodhi Society of India.

32. Mass Media

• Provisions have been allocated amounting to Rs. 400 million to the Sri Lanka Rupavahini Corporation and Rs. 489 million to the Independent TelevisionNetwork, for implementing digitalization activities, improving broadcasting facilities and purchasing modern equipment and technological tools. Rs. 384 million has been allocated to the Sri Lanka Broadcasting Corporation for Recurrent Expenditure.

• It is proposed to allocate Rs. 100 million to offer higher education scholarships and to provide support for the technological access required in journalism, in order to provide the necessary support for the skills development of journalists in keeping with the advancement in the field of mass media in parallel with modern technology.

33. Clean Sri Lanka

• The Clean Sri Lanka programme aims to transform Sri Lankan life, extending well beyond mere physical cleanliness, by steering it toward a positive direction in social, environmental and ethical terms. Under its social pillar, the programme seeks to strengthen quality of life, dignity, safety, equal access, inclusivity, community participation and social harmony; under its environmental pillar, it focuses on promoting environmental protection, conservation, stewardship, biodiversity, responsible resource utilization and sustainability and under its ethical pillar, it seeks to strengthen responsibility, honesty, discipline, fairness, efficiency and accountability regarding public resources. Rs. 6,000 million has been allocated to implement these programmes.

• Various programmes are being implemented, including: the provision and sustainable maintenance of physical infrastructure, such as “Haritha Mawatha,” “Clean Colombo,” “Clean Islands,” “Clean City,” “Clean Sri Lanka-Aththama,” the modernization of 'Dream Destination' railway stations, and the establishment of standardized public sanitation facilities; environmental development projects, including the creation of elephant nourishment zones; and initiatives to promote positive public habits, community participation, and corporate responsibility.

• Under the "Pivithuru Purawara" -Clean Cities- National Waste Management Programme, the objective is to increase the waste collection rate, currently at 32 per cent to 60 per cent by 2029, while promoting safe waste management and fostering a healthy, secure environment. It is proposed to allocate Rs. 5,000 million to implement this project across 100 selected Local Government Authorities under the “Clean Sri Lanka” initiative.

• The Clean Island project, proposed to be implemented with the support of the Ministry of Energy, will develop the islands of Delft, Nainativu and Analaitivu as “model islands” for integrated development and transformation.

34. Public Administration and Public Services

• A four-year project will be implemented to enhance the revenue-generating capacity of Local Government Institutions, utilize existing resources productively and strengthen financial sustainability. Through this, it is expected to continuously maintain the essential services of Local Government Institutions, and provision of Rs. 1,000 million has been allocated for this purpose.

• It is proposed to provide provisions of Rs. 575 million from Provincial Council funds to complete the construction of the Capital Square building, which was commenced by the Kurunegala Municipal Council and abandoned without being completed.

• It is proposed to allocate provisions of Rs. 50,000 million at Rs. 2,000 million per District to implement the District Development Programme and the Decentralized Budget Programme. Of this, Rs. 47,750 million will be allocated for special development programmes identified at District level, and the remaining Rs. 2,250 million for implementing the Decentralized Budget Programme at Rs. 10 million per Member of Parliament.

• Plans are in place to improve both roads and bridges as part of the development of the rural road network. A sum of Rs. 55,000 million has been allocated for this purpose. In addition, Rs. 5,500 million has been allocated for the improvement of rural bridges including those under the purview of Local Government Authorities while Rs. 20,000 million has been set aside for the maintenance and rehabilitation of Provincial Council roads. Accordingly, when the allocations under the District Development Programme are added to the aforementioned Rs. 80,000 million, a total of approximately Rs. 100,000 million has been earmarked for the development of rural roads.

• Rs. 1,100 million has been allocated for the construction of new District Secretariat offices in the Vavuniya, Puttalam and Anuradhapura districts with the aim of providing an efficient and quality public service to the public.

• Rs. 265 million has been allocated for the commencement of the construction of new Divisional Secretariat office buildings in Dehiattakandiya and Elapatha to further formalize public services at the regional level.

35. Public Sector Salaries and Pensions

Hon. Speaker, through our first Budget in 2025, steps were taken to increase the basic salary by increasing the minimum monthly basic salary of the public sector by Rs. 15,750 and by increasing the value of the annual salary

increment by 80 per cent. Rs. 330 billion is spent on this salary increase. This salary revision has been implemented in three phases, and its final phase will be implemented in 2027, for which Rs. 110 billion is allocated in the Budget.

Meanwhile, public servants in various sectors have submitted requests to revise their allowances, considering the increase in the related expenses they have to bear in the performance of their duties. Taking into account those requests as well as the fiscal space currently available to the Government, we expect to increase several allowances on a priority basis through this Budget. Requests to revise other allowances will be considered in phases in future Budgets, taking into account the fiscal capacity of the Government.

Accordingly, it is proposed to revise allowances as follows.

Transport Allowance of Executive Officers in the Public Service

• Executive officers who are entitled to an official vehicle under Public Administration Circular No. 05/2016 but use their private vehicles in the performance of official duties are currently paid a monthly transport allowance of Rs. 50,000. Considering the increase in maintenance, repair and other operational costs when private vehicles are used for official duties, and since it is more cost-effective for the Government to allow officers who own a vehicle to use their private vehicle rather than being provided with a Government vehicle, in order to encourage this, it is proposed to increase the monthly transport allowance currently paid to Rs. 100,000.

• With the aim of encouraging the use of electric vehicles, it is proposed to increase by 150 per cent, with effect from July, 2027, the monthly allowance paid in terms of Public Administration Circular 13/2008(V) to officers entitled to an official vehicle and who use fully electric private vehicles.

Allowances in the Health Sector

• Considering the important role they play in safeguarding the health and well-being of our people, it is proposed to increase the DAT allowance paid to medical officers by Rs. 25,000, from Rs. 70,000 to Rs. 95,000.

• To strengthen the national health system, it is proposed to increase the monthly special duty allowance of nursing officers and officers in the paramedical and supplementary medical services from Rs. 6,000 to Rs. 12,000, and the monthly special duty allowance of other health staff from Rs. 2,000 to Rs. 4,000.

• It is proposed to pay the above allowance increases proposed for the health sector in two steps. Accordingly, 50 per cent of the increase will be paid from July 2027, and the remaining 50 per cent in 2028.

405 406

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

Payments to Lecturers in Universities and Vocational Training Institutes

• Considering the important role played by university academic staff in improving the quality of higher education, promoting research activities and developing human resources, it is proposed to increase the monthly academic allowance currently paid to such staff by Rs. 25,000. It is expected to pay 50 per cent of this increase from July, 2027, and the remaining 50 per cent from July, 2028.

• In order to attract external lecturers with specialist knowledge to universities for academic activities, it is proposed to increase the payments made to external lecturers threefold in line with the existing rates. Accordingly, it is proposed to increase the allowance currently paid per hour from Rs. 1,500 to Rs. 4,500. Further, it is proposed to increase the allowance of Rs. 600 paid per hour to external advisers for practical examinations to Rs. 1,800.

• In order to attract external lecturers with specialist knowledge to vocational and technical institutions for vocational skills education, it is proposed to increase the payments made to external lecturers by 100 per cent in line with the existing rates.

Food Allowance of the Tri-Forces

• Considering the increase in the cost incurred for the food of members of the tri-forces, and with the aim of meeting their nutritional requirements and ensuring their welfare, it is proposed to increase the daily food allowance paid to the tri-forces, with effect from July, 2027, by Rs. 315 for officers and by Rs. 332 for other ranks.

Food and Accommodation Allowance of Officers of the Sri Lanka Police

• Considering the increase in food and accommodation expenses that police officers have to bear in the performance of their duties, it is proposed to increase the monthly food and accommodation allowance currently paid to meet such expenses by Rs. 10,000 with effect from July, 2027.

Risk Allowance for Prison Officers

• Taking into account the difficult and high-risk working conditions faced by prison officers in the performance of their duties, steps will be taken to increase the monthly risk allowance currently paid by 100 per cent, from Rs. 8,000 to Rs. 16,000.

• Likewise, it is proposed to increase the allowance of Rs. 2,250 currently paid to civil officers serving in the Department of Prisons to Rs. 10,000.

• It is proposed to pay these proposed allowances of officers serving in the Department of Prisons with effect from July, 2027.

Allowances for Field Officers

• Considering the role played by field officers in directly providing essential public services to the community, and the increase in the costs associated with their field-based duties, it is proposed to increase the monthly travelling allowance currently paid to Grama Niladharis from Rs. 1,600 to Rs. 4,000, and the monthly office rent allowance from Rs. 3,000 to Rs. 6,000 for Municipal Council and Urban Council areas and from Rs. 2,000 to Rs. 4,000 for Pradeshiya Sabha areas.

• The monthly travelling allowance currently paid to Development Officers will be increased from Rs. 300 to Rs. 1,000, and the monthly office rent allowance from Rs. 1,000 to Rs. 2,000 for Municipal Council and Urban Council areas and from Rs. 750 to Rs. 1,500 for Pradeshiya Sabha areas.

• The monthly travelling allowance currently paid to Samurdhi Officers will be increased from Rs. 2,000 to Rs. 2,500, and the monthly office rent allowance from Rs. 2,500 to Rs. 5,000 for Municipal Council and Urban Council areas and from Rs. 2,000 to Rs. 4,000 for Pradeshiya Sabha areas.

• It is proposed to increase the monthly travelling allowance currently paid to Agriculture Research and Production Assistants from Rs. 400 to Rs. 1,000, and the monthly office rent allowance from Rs. 3,000 to Rs. 6,000 for Municipal Council and Urban Council areas and from Rs. 2,000 to Rs. 4,000 for Pradeshiya Sabha areas.

• The above allowances for field officers will be revised from April 2027.

Revision of Allowances in the Judicial Sector

Allowances of High Court Judges and Judicial Officers

• Considering the professional responsibilities, the nature of service and the role of officers in the judicial sector, it is proposed to increase by Rs. 25,000 the monthly professional allowance currently paid to High Court Judges, Special Grade Judicial Officers and Judicial Officers of other grades. Steps will be taken to pay 50 per cent of this increase from July 2027, and the remaining 50 per cent in 2028.

Revision of Public Service Pensions

Hon. Speaker,

Pension Revision and Relief to Pensioners

• The Government is implementing a phased pension revision programme with the objective of ensuring a fair and structured pension system for all pensioners by systematically eliminating the disparities that exist among the pensions of retired public servants.

• Through the first Budget in 2025, steps were taken to convert, in three phases, the pensions of public servants who retired prior to 2020 to the 2020 basic salary scale, and its first and second phases have already been completed. Its final phase is scheduled to be implemented in 2027.

• It is also the objective of the Government to minimize and eliminate in phases, taking into account sustainable public financial management, the disparity that has arisen, due to the new basic salary revision implemented in 2025, between the pensions of public servants who retired before 2025 and those retiring in or after 2025.

• It is proposed to provide a monthly interim allowance of Rs. 2,500, with effect from 01, July 2027, as a relief to pensioners who retired during the period from 2020 to 2024, until the pension revision scheduled to be carried out under the new 2025 basic salary structure is implemented.

• The forthcoming pension revision based on the new 2025 basic salary structure is planned to commence with the 2028 Budget and to be implemented in several phases. Once this

409 410

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

revision is applied to the relevant pensioners and the payment of the revised pension commences, the payment of the above-mentioned monthly interim allowance of Rs. 2,500 proposed for those who retired during the period 2020–2024 will cease.

• These provisions will also apply to public sector pensioners whose salaries were determined under Department of Management Services Circulars 02/2025, 03/2025, 04/2025 and 05/2025.

• The overall objective of all these measures is to provide fair relief to public servants who retired at different times by systematically narrowing the pension disparities among them, and to establish a sustainable pension system in the long term.

36. Tax Policy Proposals

36.1 Increasing the Personal Income Tax Relief

and Revising the Progressive Tax Slabs

Hon. Speaker, Our Government increased the annual Personal Income Tax free threshold from Rs. 1.2 million to Rs. 1.8 million in 2025. Through this measure, individual taxpayers gained a monthly tax relief ranging from a minimum of Rs. 3,500 to a maximum of Rs. 20,500.

To provide further relief to personal income taxpayers, it is proposed to further increase the annual Personal Income Tax free threshold and to revise the Personal Income Tax structure by expanding the taxable income brackets.

We were able to increase the tax-free threshold from Rs. 100,000 per month to Rs. 150,000. Through the proposed revisions, it will be further increased to Rs. 162,500. Accordingly, a person receiving a monthly salary and allowances of up to Rs. 162,500 will not be required to pay tax on his or her salary.

The tax slab to which the 6 per cent tax rate applies will be Rs. 70,833 per month, and thereafter the tax slabs will be widened from Rs. 41,667 per month to Rs. 50,000 per month. The tax rates will be set at 6 per cent, 12 per cent, 18 percent, 24 per cent, 30 per cent and 36 per cent, applying a rate of 12 per cent instead of 18 per cent after the 6 percent rate.

These revisions will raise the monthly income threshold at which the maximum Personal Income Tax rate applies from Rs. 358,000 to Rs. 433,000. Consequently, each individual taxpayer will receive an additional monthly tax relief ranging from a minimum of Rs. 750 to a maximum of Rs. 15,750.

Accordingly, as a combined result of the tax reforms introduced in 2025 and the revisions proposed for 2027, the total monthly tax relief provided by our Government to each individual taxpayer will increase ranging from a minimum of Rs. 4,250 to a maximum of Rs. 36,250.

36.2 Enhanced Capital Allowances for Export-Oriented Manufacturing and Tourism Businesses Approved by the Board of Investment (BOI)

Hon. Speaker, with the aim of encouraging private sector investment, promoting export-oriented manufacturing and strengthening the tourism sector, it is proposed to introduce enhanced capital allowances as an investment incentive for enterprises operating under agreements with the Board of Investment of Sri Lanka.

Accordingly, BOI-approved enterprises engaged in export-oriented manufacturing or tourism development activities with a minimum qualifying investment of US Dollars 3 million will be permitted subject to meeting the relevant requirements to opt for an enhanced capital allowance equivalent to 200 per cent of qualifying capital expenditure in place of the existing allowance regime.

36.3 Exemption of Exercise Books and Printed Books from Value Added Tax –VAT

Hon. Speaker, printed books and other printed materials became subject to Value Added Tax – VAT - effective from January 2024, while exercise books had been subject to VAT even before that date.

Recognizing the importance of education and the necessity of ensuring that essential educational materials are available at an affordable price, and with the objective of promoting access to education, knowledge and reading, we propose to exempt textbooks, dictionaries, encyclopaedias, children's drawing and colouring books, educational publications and other printed books, as well as exercise books, from Value Added Tax -VAT.

36.4 Revising the Basis for Calculating VAT

Liability on Cinema Ticket Revenue

With the aim of ensuring the fair and equitable application of Value Added Tax within the film industry, we propose to revise the basis for calculating the VAT liability arising from the sale of cinema tickets.

Accordingly, the VAT liability of film producers, distributors, the National Film Corporation and cinema hall operators will be determined on the basis of the share of cinema ticket revenue received by or allocated to each party at each stage, rather than calculating VAT on the full ticket value.

36.5 Facilitating Earlier Qualification of Newly VAT Registered Exporters as “Eligible Exporters”

Hon. Speaker, with the aim of facilitating export-oriented businesses, I propose to revise the criteria under which newly VAT registered exporters are recognized as Eligible Exporters.

Accordingly, newly VAT registered persons will be recognized as Eligible Exporters where more than 80 per cent of their turnover during the preceding six months has

411 412

[ගරු අනුර කුමොර දිසො ොයක මහතො ]

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

been derived from exports, without having to satisfy the existing requirement relating to the preceding calendar year.

36.6 Use of the Indicative Exchange Rate of the

Central Bank of Sri Lanka for Value Added Tax on Foreign Currency Supplies

Hon. Speaker, in order to ensure greater transparency, consistency and fairness in valuing taxable supplies denominated in foreign currencies, it is proposed to use the Indicative Exchange Rate published by the Central Bank of Sri Lanka for the conversion of foreign currencies into Sri Lanka Rupees for VAT purposes. The Commissioner General of Inland Revenue –CGIR- will specify by way of a circular the manner in which such exchange rate is to be applied.

36.7 Social Security Contribution Levy –SSCL -

Exemption for Bunker Fuel, Supplied to Ships Engaged in International Transportation

Hon. Speaker, the supply of bunker fuel to ships engaged in international transport is an important component of Sri Lanka's maritime services sector. This industry operates in a highly competitive international market.

With the aim of safeguarding the competitiveness of the industry and strengthening Sri Lanka's position as a regional maritime hub, we propose to exempt the supply of fuel to ships engaged in international transport from the Social Security Contribution Levy.

36.8 Review and Rationalization of the Tax

Structure applicable to the Betting and Gaming Industry

Hon. Speaker, addressing the long-standing need for an effective regulatory framework for the betting and gaming industry, the Government has established the Gambling Regulatory Authority – GRA - and commenced its operations.

Given that a regulatory framework is now in place, it is proposed to conduct a comprehensive review of the taxes, fees, and charges applicable to this industry with the view to eliminate instances of double taxation and inconsistencies, align the tax structure with international best practices, and ensure a fair and balanced approach that supports the sustainable development of the industry while safeguarding government revenue.

This review aims to establish a simple, transparent, and integrated tax structure that is easy to administer and facilitates compliance.

36.9 Strengthening Tax Administration and Facilitating Taxpayer Compliance

Hon. Speaker, with the objective of strengthening tax administration, enhancing legal certainty, and facilitating tax compliance for taxpayers, we propose to remove

ambiguities present in certain provisions of existing tax laws. Furthermore, we propose to review and amend these provisions to align them with evolving business practices and new forms of transactions.

Additionally, we propose to review certain stringent procedural requirements which could lead to taxpayers denied legitimate tax refunds or input tax credits due technical or procedural noncompliance while ensuring that adequate safeguards remain in place to protect government revenue.

These measures aim to establish a fair, transparent, and efficient tax administration system that encourages voluntary tax compliance, safeguards the taxpayers’ legitimate entitlements, and protects Government revenue.

36.10 Revision of the Special Commodity Levy

(SCL) on Several Essential Food Items

Hon. Speaker, with the aim of minimizing the cost-of-living pressure on consumers caused by rising prices of essential food items, it is proposed to reduce the Special Commodity Levy on imported sprats from Rs. 100 to Rs. 10 per kilogram, on Maldive fish from Rs. 302 to Rs. 10 per kilogram, and on powdered and unpowdered dried chillies by Rs. 50 per kilogram.

These tax revisions are scheduled to be implemented with effect from November 13, 2026.

36.11 Phased Reduction of Import Tariffs

Hon. Speaker, it is proposed to reduce by 25 per cent, the existing CESS rates applicable to 800 selected Harmonized System (HS) code lines, under the Government's programme of phased reduction of import tariffs with the aim of simplifying the Import Tax structure, reducing trade-related costs and enhancing the competitiveness of the economy.

It is proposed to remove the 10 per cent Ports and Airports Development Levy –PAL- currently charged on the importation of newsprint classified under specified HS codes, with effect from January 1, 2027.

36.12 Supporting the Domestic Sugar Industry

to Compete in the Market

To protect the domestic sugar industry, it is also proposed to increase the Special Commodity Levy imposed on imported sugar by Rs. 10 per kilogram, in order to minimize the tax disparity between domestically produced sugar and imported sugar, create equal competitive opportunities and a fair competitive environment for both parties, and ensure a more neutral and equitable tax treatment.

36.13 Abolition of the Tourism Development Levy

Hon. Speaker, with the aim of reducing the tax burden on the tourism industry, enhancing its competitiveness and ensuring sustainable financing for tourism development, it is proposed to abolish the Tourism

413 414

පොර්ිනම්න්තුව

Development Levy currently charged on the turnover of tourism establishments.

In order to ensure continued funding for tourism promotion and development, steps will be taken in the future to formulate a suitable financing mechanism. Until then, the required funds will be provided by the Treasury on requests made by the Sri Lanka Tourism Development Authority.

36.14 Amendments to the Excise Ordinance

The 2025 Budget proposed to introduce a new Excise Act in place of the century-old Excise Ordinance with the aim of improving revenue management.

However, until a new Act is introduced, we propose to bring amendments to the Excise Ordinance by further simplifying this complex process and incorporating amendments into the existing Ordinance itself.

37. Borrowing Limit

The borrowing limit of the Appropriation Bill for the financial year 2027 is presented in Annexure IV. Summary of New Expenditure Proposals Presented in the 2027 Budget is given in Annexure II. Further, the documents to be presented with the Second Reading of the Budget under the Public Financial Management Act, No. 44 of 2024 have also been tabled.

38. Supplementary Estimate for Ditwah Reconstruction – 2027

It is proposed to allocate as provisions for 2027 the amount that has not been utilized so far, due to reasons including the war situation in the Middle East, out of the Supplementary Estimate of Rs. 500 billion approved by Parliament for the reconstruction of Ditwah damage. Budgetary provisions of Rs. 260 billion have to be secured again for 2027 to properly complete the Ditwah reconstruction activities.

Accordingly, in addition to the 2027 Budget I have presented, it is proposed to present to Parliament a Supplementary Estimate for Ditwah reconstruction expenditure in accordance with the provisions of Section 16 of the Public Financial Management Act.

It is proposed to table that Supplementary Estimate today, following the Budget Speech.

Conclusion

Hon. Speaker, our Budget Proposals are not confined to paper alone. They are Proposals that are implemented in practice on the ground. Therefore, every new proposal will have a clearly accountable institution, outcome indicators, national priorities, a cash flow plan and transparency that can be easily observed by the public.

What we are striving to build is a country that safeguards the dignity of the citizen, ensures the safety of the child, gives due value to labour, creates opportunities for knowledge and protects natural resources for future generations.

Having overcome the challenges that prevailed and built stability in the economy, our next target is to commence a forward-looking and transformative era in 2027. For this, the collective strength and contribution of all of you is essential to us.

Hon. Speaker, in the modern world, governments are changed through progressive ideological revolutions, through the intelligent decisions of the people and through advanced policies and programmes suited to the future. However, our Opposition is still clinging to superstitious beliefs and has been unable to free itself from regressive thinking.

Politics must be done by going among the people on the ground, not by tying threads in cemeteries at night, nor by wasting the time of Parliament reciting curse verses.

Likewise, read the Kalama Sutta, which the Lord Buddha preached to the Kalamas -the residents of the town of Kesaputta-, pointing out that nothing should be accepted blindly but should be examined with wisdom, and understand the revolution the Lord Buddha made against blind faith. Read the Aggañña Sutta, which the Lord Buddha preached against superstition, and the Kevatta Sutta, preached against false incantations, and understand the ideological revolution brought about by the Lord Buddha.

In the Tevijja Sutta, the Lord Buddha asks whether, if a man standing on this bank of a river, wishing to go to the other bank, neither swims nor uses a boat but prays to the far bank, saying "Come here, far bank, come here", the far bank would come to this side.

Hon. Speaker, we have a very clear, progressive and proud dream for our Motherland. Our hope is to make this country not merely an island, but one of the most advanced countries, not only in Asia but in the whole world, rich in knowledge, skills and modern technology.

Today, we are implementing a strong programme towards that end. We have commenced thetransformationofmaking ouryoungergeneration andour children across the country fully skilled, advanced citizens by providing them with modern technology, science and global knowledge.

Hon. Speaker, what we need is not only to build an economically developed country, but also to build a new image of the Sri Lankan before the world. Our dream is to brand Sri Lanka in the world in such a way that a Sri Lankan is recognized as an educated, intelligent, civilized, respectable and trustworthy citizen. Our expectation is to mark before the world that, though we are a small country, our human resources are endowed

with a unique intelligence. We want to prove to the whole world that we have a generation of citizens who fulfil the responsibilities they have undertaken, work with dedication, create new ideas, find solutions in the face of challenges and can move forward shoulder to shoulder with the world. What we are trying to build is not only a new economy for Sri Lanka, but also a new recognition, a new respect and a new pride for the Sri Lankan before the world.

But bring alternative policies instead of curse verses, creative solutions instead of sorcery, and intelligent dialogue instead of yaga and homa rituals into the politics of this country. When the people of our country are moving forward with technology and intelligence, should the politics of the Opposition alone turn upside down and go backwards? I would like to remind at this moment that building a political culture that befits the civilized, enlightened and proud Sri Lankan identity we are trying to build before the world is a fundamental responsibility entrusted not only to the Government, but also to the Opposition. The intelligent people of this country will never again set foot in that old darkness.

Hon. Speaker, what we must leave behind for future generations is not our party disputes, but a prosperous and just country the future of which they can take over with confidence. Therefore, let us make the name "Sri Lankan" not merely a name we pronounce with pride, but a proud name that the whole world accepts with respect.

Let us prove our pledge towards that, not merely in words, but through action.

Today, we extend an invitation with an open heart to all political parties in this country, to all stakeholders, and to every Sri Lankan living anywhere in the world. Come, let us become partners in building the future of this

country! We do not need to think along the same lines; we do not need to hold the same opinion. But our country is greater than our differences. The future of our children is more valuable than our individual goals. Therefore, let us bring together our knowledge, our abilities and our strengths. Let us begin from where we can agree. Let us unite, not to get ahead of one another, but to take our Motherland ahead of us all!

In the end, what we must leave behind for our children is not the story of how far above one another we stood, but the story of how high we all, together, raised this country.

The victory of that story will not belong to one individual; it will not belong to one party; it will not belong to one generation alone.

That victory will belong to every citizen of this country. That victory must have only one name – Sri Lanka!

Finally, I express my sincere gratitude to all the officials of the Ministry of Finance and the Secretary to the Treasury, Dr. Harshana Suriyapperuma, who worked with unwavering dedication and perseverance in preparing this Budget. Their professionalism, dedication and sense of duty have been instrumental in the preparation of this comprehensive and forward-looking Budget.

As we collectively advance the Government's economic reform agenda and national development goals, we always look forward to your continued support and commitment as officials of the Ministry of Finance to ensure and implement these Proposals in a timely and effective manner.

Thank you.

2026 න ොවැම්බර් 12

ගරු කථානායකතුමා (மாண்புமிகு சபாநாயகர் அவர்கள்) (The Hon. Speaker) විසර්ජ ප ත් නකටුම්පත 2027, නෙව වර කියවීනම් විවොෙය කල් තැබීම, ගරු සභො ොයකුමො.

ගරු බිමල් රත්නායක මහතා (மாண்புமிகு பிமல் ரத்நாயக்க) (The Hon. Bimal Rathnayake) ගරු කථො ොයකුමනි, "විවොෙය ෙැන්ත කල් තැබිය යුුය"යි මො නයෝජ ො කර වො.

ප්‍රශ්නනය ිමමසන ලින්, සභා සේමත ිමය வினா விடுக்கப்பட்டு ஏற்றுக்ககாள்ேப்பட்ைது. Question put, and agreed to. ඊට අවාකූලව ිමවාදය කල් තබන ලදී එතැ්, සි අට ිමවාදය 2026 මනාවැේබර් 13වන සි අකුරාදා පවත්වවා ලැමේ

இதன்படி விவாதம் ஒத்திடவக்கப்பட்ைது. விவாதம், 2026 நவம்பர் 13, கவள்ேிக்கிழடம மீேத் கதாைங்கும். The Debate stood adjourned accordingly. Debate to be resumed on ‍Friday, 13th November, 2026.

ගරු කථානායකතුමා (மாண்புமிகு சபாநாயகர் அவர்கள்) (The Hon. Speaker) 2027 විසර්ජ ප ත් නකටුම්පතට අෙොළව කොරක සභො අවස්ථොනේදී නයෝජ ො කිරීමට නියමිත සියලුම සංන ෝධ 2026 න ොවැම්බර් 16 සඳුෙො අපර භොග 3.00ට නහෝ ඊට ප්‍රථම පොර්ිනම්න්තුනේ මහ නල්කම්වරිය නවත භොර දිය යුු බව ෙැනුම් දීමට කැමැත්නතමි.

ගරු අග්‍රොමොතය සහ අධයොප , සසස් අධයොප සහ ව්ත්ීය අධයොප අමොතයුමිය.

ගරු (ආචාර්ය) හරිනි අමරසූරිය (අග්‍රාමාතය සහ අ්‍යාපන උසසන අ්‍යාපන සහ ව්ත් ය අ්‍යාපන අමාතයතුියය) (மாண்புமிகு (கலாநிதி) ஹாினி அமரசூாிய - பிரதம அடமச்சரும் கல்வி, உயர் கல்வி மற்றும் கதாழிற்கல்வி அடமச்சரும்) (The Hon. (Dr.) Harini Amarasuriya - Prime Minister and Minister of Education, Higher Education and Vocational Education) Hon. Speaker, notwithstanding the provisions of Standing Order No. 22 of Parliament, with special leave of the House, I present the Supplementary Estimate for the year 2027 submitted under Sections 15‍and 16‍of the Public Financial Management Act, No. 44‍of 2024.

I move that the Supplementary Estimate be referred to the Committee on Public Finance.

ප්‍රශ්නනය ිමමසන ලින්, සභා සේමත ිමය வினா விடுக்கப்பட்டு ஏற்றுக்ககாள்ேப்பட்ைது. Question put, and agreed to.

කල්තැබීම ஒத்திடவப்பு ADJOURNMENT

ගරු බිමල් රත්නායක මහතා (மாண்புமிகு பிமல் ரத்நாயக்க) (The Hon. Bimal Rathnayake) ගරු කථො ොයකුමනි, "පොර්ිනම්න්තුව ෙැන්ත කල් තැබිය යුුය" යි මො නයෝජ ො කර වො.

ප්‍රශ්නනය ිමමසන ලින්, සභා සේමත ිමය வினா விடுக்கப்பட்டு ஏற்றுக்ககாள்ேப்பட்ைது. Question put, and agreed to. පාර්ිමේ්,තුව ඊට අවාකූලව අ භා 6 31ට 2026 ඔකමතෝබර් 23වන ිනන සභා සේමතිය අවාව 2026 මනාවැේබර් 13වන සි අකුරාදා පූ භා 9 30 වන මතක කල් ගිමේය

அதன்படி, பி.ப. 6.31 மணிக்கு பாராளுமன்றம், அதனது 2026‍ஒக்ளராபர் 23ஆம் திகதிய தீர்மானத்திற்கிணங்க, 2026 நவம்பர் 13, கவள்ேிக்கிழடம மு.ப. 9.30 மணிவடர ஒத்திடவக்கப்பட்ைது.

Parliament adjourned accordingly at 6.31 p.m. until 9.30 a.m. on Friday, 13th November, 2026, pursuant to the Resolution of Parliament of 23rd October, 2026.

සැ යු

නමම වොර්තොනේ අවසො මුද්‍රණයය සහහො ස්වීයය කථොව නිවැරදි කළ යුු තැන්ත ේවනු රිසි මන්තත්‍රීන්ත මින්ත ටපටපත් නග

නිවැරදි කළ යුු ආකොරය එහි පැහැදිිව කුණු නකොට, ටපටපත ැබී නෙසතිය් න ොඉ්මවො

හැ්,සාඩ් සංස්කොරක නවත ැනබ නස් එවිය යුුය.

குைிப்பு

உறுப்பினர் தமது உடரயின் இறுதிப் பதிப்பிற் கசய்யவிரும்பும் பிடழ திருத்தங்கடே தமது பிரதியில் கதேிவாகக் குறித்து

அதடனப் பிடழ திருத்தப்பைாத பிரதி கிடைத்த இரு வாரங்களுள் ஹன்சாட் பதிப்பாசிாியருக்கு அனுப்புதல் ளவண்டும்.
